NT$10,000 may be added to the maximum subsidy limits listed below, subject to the
funding availability of the current year:
(1) Cross-disciplinary Credit Programs: Maximum of NT$50,000.
(2) Cross-disciplinary Micro-programs: Maximum of NT$20,000.
3. The top 3 credit programs with the highest number of certificates issued per
academic year will be awarded certificates of appreciation and subsidy funds as
follows:
* **Rank 1:** Credit Program Subsidy Limit: NT$50,000; Cross-disciplinary Micro-
program Subsidy Limit: NT$30,000.
* **Rank 2:** Credit Program Subsidy Limit: NT$40,000; Cross-disciplinary Micro-
program Subsidy Limit: NT$20,000.
* **Rank 3:** Credit Program Subsidy Limit: NT$30,000; Cross-disciplinary Micro-
program Subsidy Limit: NT$10,000.
II. Funding Subsidy Items: Including operating expenses and capital expenses.
1. Operating Expenses: Includes lecturer hourly fees, attendance fees, domestic travel
expenses, short-distance fares, insurance premiums, supplementary premiums for
Second Generation National Health Insurance, printing costs, meal expenses, item
expenses, etc., required for preparing/opening credit programs, offering courses, or
meetings; as well as student labor wages for lecture activities, grants for students
(e.g., Teaching Assistants, rewards for students obtaining certificates), miscellaneous
expenses, etc.
2. Capital Expenses: Software and hardware facilities costing over NT$10,000 with a
useful life of more than 2 years (must be primarily for enhancing teaching resources).
### Page 3
III. Subsidized units shall budget and verify expenses in accordance with the
"Ministry of Education Operation Guidelines for Subsidy and Commissioned Funding
Verification" and handle expense verification matters according to the University's
internal procedures.
Article 5 (Others)
Subsidized credit programs must submit outcome data to the Curriculum Section
before the end of December each year and cooperate with the University's various
management, evaluation, and promotion matters, providing relevant data and
participating in relevant curriculum meetings, result exhibitions, and presentations.
However, credit programs may be exempt from attaching outcome data for the "Top 3
Subsidy Funds for Number of Certificates Issued per Academic Year."
Article 6 (Funding Source)
Funded by the Ministry of Education's Higher Education Sprout Project.
Article 7 (Supplementary Provisions)
These Guidelines shall be implemented after being approved by the Academic Affairs
Meeting and ratified by the President; the same applies to amendments.

## National Taipei University Guidelines for the Establishment of the Artificial Intelligence (AI) Credit Program Committee (Rev. Oct. 2024)
### Page 1
National Taipei University Guidelines for the Establishment of the Artificial
Intelligence (AI) Credit Program Committee
In case of any discrepancy or inconsistency between the English and Chinese
versions of the regulations, the Chinese version shall take precedence.
Last Revised: October 2024
Article 1 These Guidelines are established to respond to the urgent industry demand for
artificial intelligence talent and to cooperate with the Ministry of Education's
Taiwan Alliance of Institutes for Artificial Intelligence (TAICA, hereinafter
referred to as the "AI Alliance") plan, for the purpose of executing matters
related to artificial intelligence credit programs under said plan.
Article 2 The Artificial Intelligence (AI) Credit Program Committee (hereinafter
referred to as "the Committee") shall consist of several members. The
Convener shall be recommended by the Office of Academic Affairs and
appointed by the President. The remaining members shall consist of one
teacher representative recommended by each College of the University, one
external scholar or expert, one representative from the Curriculum Section,
one representative from the Center for Teaching and Learning Development,
and one representative from the Information Center. Relevant units may be
invited to attend as non-voting delegates when necessary. The term of office
for members of the Committee is one year, ending at the conclusion of the
academic year. Members of the Committee are unpaid positions; however,
external members may be paid attendance fees and transportation expenses.
Article 3 The tasks of the Committee are as follows: I. Responsible for executing credit
programs planned by the AI Alliance and other matters related to artificial
intelligence credit programs, including revising guidelines for establishing
credit programs and subject planning tables. 1. AI Exploration and
Application Credit Program 2. AI Industrial Application Credit Program 3.
AI Natural Language Technology Credit Program 4. AI Visual Technology
Credit Program II. Inventory of regular on-campus courses: Based on the
program plan provided by the AI Alliance Project Office, conduct an
inventory of on-campus courses that can be recognized, and add courses
approved by the AI Alliance Project Office review to the aforementioned
credit program subject planning tables. III. Planning the offering of satellite
courses: Assist in consulting relevant course-offering departments/institutes
### Page 2
and coordinating on-campus teachers to support the offering of satellite
courses. IV. Assist in reviewing student course-taking and certification: 1. If
students apply to waive courses in the aforementioned credit program plans
with elective courses of similar nature (content of the waived course and
proof of grades must be provided), the waiver may only be granted after
applying to the Curriculum Section and receiving approval from the
Committee review. 2. After students apply for certification, upon review and
approval by the Committee, the University-level administrative unit shall
issue the internal credit program certificate in accordance with the
University's operational procedures, or the Curriculum Section shall request
the AI Alliance Project Office to issue the Ministry of Education AI Alliance
digital certificate. V. Integrate the University's cross-disciplinary AI
curriculum resources and assist in promoting matters related to cross-
disciplinary AI credit programs.
Article 4 The Committee may form subgroups based on task requirements. It shall
convene at least one meeting per semester, and extraordinary meetings may
be held when necessary.
Article 5 These Guidelines shall be implemented after being ratified by the President;
the same applies to amendments.

## National Taipei University Regulations for Student Internships (Rev. Aprl. 2025)
### Page 1
National Taipei University Regulations for Student Internships
In case of any discrepancy or inconsistency between the English and Chinese versions of the
regulations, the Chinese version shall take precedence.
Last Revised: April 2025
Article 1 These Regulations are established in accordance with Article 12 of the Technical and Vocational
Education Act, Article 6 of the Regulations Governing the Implementation of Industry-Academia
Cooperation in Junior Colleges and Above, and Article 73 of the National Taipei University
Academic Regulations to enhance students' professional practical abilities and safeguard their
internship rights.
Article 2 Student internships at the University include: I. Professional Internship Courses: Each teaching
unit may plan and implement on-campus and off-campus internship courses tailored to the
development characteristics of the department/institute and curriculum objectives, combining
academic professionalism with skills required for students' careers. This enables students to
experience the workplace early, establish correct work attitudes, and stimulate learning and
future career planning. II. Non-course-based Internships: The Career Development Center of the
Office of Student Affairs serves as the information dissemination window, providing students
with information and matchmaking opportunities for voluntary on-campus and off-campus
workplace experiences.
Administrative affairs for the internships mentioned in the preceding paragraph shall be
coordinated by the Office of Academic Affairs and the Office of Student Affairs respectively,
according to the content and nature of the internship.
Article 3 To manage student internship affairs, a University-level Student Internship Committee
(hereinafter referred to as "the Committee") is established. The Vice President for Academic
Affairs shall serve as the Chairperson. The Dean of Academic Affairs, Dean of Student Affairs,
Dean of International Affairs, Director of the Division of Extension and Continuing Education,
and Deans of each College shall be ex-officio members. One teacher representative elected by
each College shall serve as an elected member. One student representative from each College
recommended by the Student Association, one external legal scholar or expert, and three
representatives from external internship institutions shall be appointed by the President upon the
request of the Chairperson. The term of office for ex-officio members shall correspond to their
administrative term. The term of office for elected members, the external legal scholar or expert,
and representatives from external internship institutions is two years. The term for student
representatives recommended by the Student Association is one year.
When the University-level Student Internship Committee meets, the Director of the Center for
Teaching and Learning Development, Director of the Career Development Center, Chief of the
Curriculum Section, Chief of the Registration Section, and Chief of the Extension Education
Section shall attend as non-voting delegates.
### Page 2
The Committee shall convene once every academic year in principle; extraordinary meetings may
be held when necessary. Relevant units or personnel may be invited to attend to report or
provide explanations.
Article 4 The tasks of the University-level Student Internship Committee are as follows: I. Supervise the
assessment and selection of cooperative institutions. II. Review and confirm written contracts.
III. Evaluate the effectiveness of university-wide internships and supervise the handling of
student grievances, disputes, and accidents. IV. Supervise the handling of internship termination
before the expiration of the internship period. V. Supervise the formulation of individual student
internship plans with cooperative institutions. VI. Supervise the implementation of internship
guidance visits. VII. Other matters related to the protection of student rights.
Article 5 For off-campus internships, the implementation methods, internship locations, faculty, credit
recognition, guidance, and other regulations regarding the protection of students' off-campus
internship rights shall be determined by each executing unit, unless otherwise stipulated by law.
Each executing unit shall establish a College, Department, Institute, Center, or Degree Program
Student Internship Committee to assist in the planning and implementation of off-campus
internship courses to ensure students' rights during off-campus internships.
The composition of the College, Department, Institute, Center, or Degree Program Student
Internship Committee mentioned in the preceding paragraph shall be determined by each
executing unit, and its tasks are as follows: I. Overall planning and promotion of off-campus
internship courses. II. Confirm the assessment results and selection of cooperative institutions.
III. Draft written contracts and individual student internship plans. IV. Coordinate and handle
student grievances, disputes, and accidents. V. Handle the termination of internships before the
expiration of the internship period. VI. Track, handle, and review the results of student
internship guidance visits. VII. Other matters related to the protection of student rights.
Article 6 Off-campus internship institutions for students shall be rigorously screened and evaluated by each
executing unit. Off-campus internship institutions recommended by students must also be
approved by the executing unit before the internship can proceed.
Article 7 Each executing unit shall sign a written cooperation contract with the internship institution,
stating the rights and obligations of both parties to safeguard student rights, including: I. The
cooperative institution shall provide students with relevant practical training in accordance with
the individual student internship plan and jointly guide the students with the specialized
guidance teacher assigned by the University. II. The cooperative institution is responsible for
safety training before the internship, the allocation of safety protection equipment at the
internship site, and the planning of relevant safety measures. III. Insure relevant insurance for
intern students. IV. Clearly specify the internship duration (daily learning hours, leave or holiday
regulations), contract period, internship content, internship scholarship or salary payment,
accommodation and transportation, and performance assessment criteria. V. Coordination and
handling methods for disputes between the cooperative institution and intern students. VI.
### Page 3
Conditions and procedures for termination or dissolution before the expiration of the student
internship.
If students provide labor or engage in work facts other than learning and training at the
cooperative institution during the internship period, the written contract shall be handled in
accordance with the Labor Standards Act.
Article 8 Teacher education internship courses shall be handled separately in accordance with the Teacher
Education Act and relevant regulations. Social work internship courses shall be handled
separately in accordance with the Senior Professional and Technical Examinations Regulations
for Social Workers and relevant regulations.
Article 9 These Regulations do not apply to autonomous off-campus internships arranged by students
themselves.
Article 10 These Regulations shall be implemented after being approved by the University Council and
ratified by the President; the same applies to amendments.

## National Taipei University Regulations for Establishing Credit Programs (Rev. Oct. 2021)
### Page 1
National Taipei University Regulations for Establishing Credit Programs
In case of any discrepancy or inconsistency between the English and Chinese versions of the
regulations, the Chinese version shall take precedence.
Last Revised: October 2021
Article 1 These Regulations are established in accordance with Article 11 of the University Act and Articles
8 through 11 of the Enforcement Rules of the University Act to encourage students to take cross-
disciplinary courses and broaden their horizons.
Article 2 The various teaching and research units of National Taipei University (hereinafter referred to as
"the University") may establish cross-school, cross-college, cross-department, or cross-institute
credit programs. The term "credit program" in the preceding paragraph refers to a professional
curriculum design and combination across schools, colleges, departments, and institutes that
grants a credit certificate. Cross-school cooperative credit programs are limited to schools that
have signed an educational strategic alliance agreement with the University and schools within the
University System of Taipei. The University may authorize teaching and research units to
negotiate relevant matters such as curriculum planning and implementation.
Article 3 The establishment, modification, or termination of a credit program shall be approved by the
University Curriculum Committee and then announced for implementation; credit programs in
special professional fields involving the government's manpower cultivation quota control
mechanism shall also be reported to the Ministry of Education for reference.
The establishment regulations of a credit program shall include items such as the objective, name,
curriculum plan, number of credits, eligibility, number limits, application and approval
procedures, and a convener jointly recommended by relevant departments/institutes.
The curriculum planning for credit programs is as follows: I. Cross-disciplinary Credit Programs:
Curriculum planning is oriented towards cultivating students' cross-disciplinary abilities, with a
minimum of 15 credits. II. Micro-programs: 1. Cross-disciplinary Micro-programs: Curriculum
planning is oriented towards facilitating student ability development and future employment, with
a minimum of 8 credits. 2. Single-discipline Micro-programs: For micro-programs where the
target students are "students not belonging to the course-offering unit," the curriculum planning
may be exempt from the restrictions of Paragraphs 1 and 2 of Article 2 of these Regulations.
Article 4 Each College shall integrate all credit programs within the college and assist with relevant
administrative affairs of each credit program. The credit program convener shall coordinate and
handle program-related affairs: I. The convener shall review and evaluate the appropriateness of
the existing program curriculum planning at least once every academic year, and effectively
utilize consultation from external industry experts, scholars, alumni, etc. II. When planning
courses, coordination shall first be made with the course-offering department or instructor. Course
offering situations shall be reviewed regularly every semester to confirm the offering cycle. If
### Page 2
required courses for the credit program are not offered, they shall be made up in the summer
session or a substitute plan shall be planned to facilitate students in completing the credit program.
III. Before the termination of a program, supporting measures to guide students in completing the
credit program shall be planned: 1. The deadline for student application for accreditation shall be
announced. The deadline shall be at least 2 years calculated from the semester in which the
University Curriculum Committee approves the termination. 2. Before the deadline for student
accreditation application, course offerings shall be continued stably, or substitute plans for credit
waiver shall be planned.
Article 5 To improve the teaching quality and effectiveness of credit program courses, credit programs shall
be evaluated once every four years. The evaluation regulations shall be prescribed separately.
Article 6 (Deleted)
Article 7 These Regulations do not apply to the Teacher Education Program, which shall be handled in
accordance with relevant regulations of the University's Teacher Education Program.
Article 8 Matters not provided for in these Regulations shall be handled in accordance with the University's
Academic Regulations and relevant laws and regulations of the Ministry of Education.
Article 9 These Regulations shall be implemented after being approved by the Academic Affairs Meeting
and ratified by the President; the same applies to amendments.

## National Taipei University Regulations for the Establishment of Curriculum Committees at All Levels (Rev. Dec. 2021)
### Page 1
National Taipei University Regulations for the Establishment of
Curriculum Committees at All Levels
In case of any discrepancy or inconsistency between the English and Chinese
versions of the regulations, the Chinese version shall take precedence.
Last Revised: December 2021
Article 1 These Regulations are established in accordance with Article 24 of the
Enforcement Rules of the University Act and Article 12 of the National
Taipei University Organizational Regulations.
Article 2 The University's curriculum committees are divided into three levels:
University, College, and Department (Institute, Center, Office). The
University-level Curriculum Committee is established in accordance with
these Regulations; the regulations for the establishment of College and
Department (Institute, Center, Office) level curriculum committees shall be
formulated by the respective Colleges and Departments (Institutes, Centers,
Offices). The members of curriculum committees at all levels shall include
at least one external scholar or expert.
Article 3 When curriculum committees at all levels of the University deliberate on
curriculum planning, student representatives shall attend and participate in
the discussion. Matters that affect the graduation credits and credit
calculations as stipulated in Article 26, Paragraph 2 of the University Act
shall be announced to students.
Article 4 The University-level Curriculum Committee is responsible for planning and
establishing guidelines for the curriculum, credits, and content of each
Department (Institute, Center, Office), as well as deliberating and
coordinating related matters.
Article 5 The University-level Curriculum Committee shall have the Dean of
Academic Affairs as the Chairperson and Convener. The Director of the
Division of Extension and Continuing Education, Deans of each College,
and Directors of the Physical Education Office, Military Training Office,
and Center for General Education shall be ex-officio members. One teacher
representative elected by each College-level curriculum committee each
academic year shall serve as an elected member. External scholars or
### Page 2
experts shall be appointed by the President upon the request of the
Convener.
When the University-level Curriculum Committee meets, the Chiefs of the
Curriculum Section, Registration Section, and Extension Education
Section, as well as the Directors of the Language Center and the Center for
Teaching and Learning Development, shall attend as non-voting delegates.
The Student Association shall recommend one student representative from
each College every academic year to attend and participate in discussions.
The Chairperson may also invite heads of relevant academic units or full-
time teachers to attend as non-voting delegates when necessary.
Article 6 The term of office for ex-officio members of the University-level
Curriculum Committee shall correspond to their administrative term. The
term of office for elected members is one year, ending at the conclusion of
the academic year.
Article 7 The University-level Curriculum Committee shall convene at least once
every academic year.
Article 8 (Deleted)
Article 9 These Regulations shall be implemented after being approved by the
University Council and ratified by the President; the same applies to
amendments.

## National Taipei University Student Examination Rules (Rev. Oct. 2021)
### Page 1
National Taipei University Student Examination Rules
In case of any discrepancy or inconsistency between the English and Chinese
versions of the regulations, the Chinese version shall take precedence.
Last Revised: October 2021
Article 1 These Rules are established in accordance with Article 52 of the National
Taipei University Academic Regulations.
Article 2 Students shall obey the instructions of the proctors.
Article 3 Students may not leave the examination room within 30 minutes after the
start of the examination. Students who are more than 20 minutes late are
not allowed to enter the examination room.
Article 4 Except for reference materials designated by the instructor and necessary
stationery, students are not allowed to bring books, handouts, or any other
items into the examination room.
Article 5 Students must bring their Student ID and place it on the upper left corner of
the desk for inspection. Those without a Student ID shall be stopped from
taking the examination by the proctor and ordered to leave the room
immediately.
Article 6 Students shall sit in the designated rows from front to back and sign the
attendance sheet respectively.
Article 7 Students shall fill in their name, student ID number, department (institute)
and grade, subject and section, and instructor's name on the answer sheet.
Article 8 The answer sheets distributed to students have a fixed number of pages and
must not be torn arbitrarily.
Article 9 If the handwriting on the question paper is unclear and students have
questions, they should raise their hand first. They are not allowed to knock
on the desk or leave their seats. Questions are limited to the first 20 minutes
of the examination.
### Page 2
Article 10 Students are not allowed to talk, peek at others' answer sheets, or
intentionally facilitate peeking by neighbors. Violators will receive a grade
of zero for that subject.
Article 11 Students found passing notes, copying, or smuggling notes, etc., shall be
ordered to leave the room immediately. The subject will be graded as zero,
and they will be penalized according to relevant university regulations
depending on the severity of the offense.
Article 12 In cases of impersonation, both parties involved in the cheating shall be
expelled.
Article 13 Students who are unable to take the examination due to illness or major
accidents must apply for leave within one week after the examination in
accordance with the National Taipei University Student Leave Rules.
Article 14 Students who are absent without applying for leave or whose leave is not
approved shall receive a grade of zero for the missed subject.
Students granted examination leave shall take a make-up examination. The
time and method of the make-up examination shall be determined by the
instructor. However, the schedule for the instructor to submit and correct
grades shall be handled in accordance with the National Taipei University
Regulations for Instructors Submitting and Correcting Grades.
Students who leave the room without submitting their answer sheet shall
receive a grade of zero for the subject and shall be penalized according to
relevant university regulations depending on the severity of the offense.
Article 15 Students shall not ask others to submit their answer sheets on their behalf.
Violators and the person submitting on their behalf shall both receive a
grade of zero for that subject.
Article 16 Students who engage in other improper behaviors not stipulated in these
Rules shall be penalized according to relevant university regulations
depending on the severity of the offense.
Article 17 These Rules shall be implemented after being approved by the Academic
Affairs Meeting and ratified by the President; the same applies to
amendments.

## National Taipei University Regulations for Combined Bachelor's and Master's Degree Programs (Rev. Oct. 2022)
### Page 1
National Taipei University Regulations for Combined Bachelor's and Master's
Degree Programs
In case of any discrepancy or inconsistency between the English and Chinese
versions of the regulations, the Chinese version shall take precedence.
Last Revised: October 2022
Article 1 These Regulations are established to encourage outstanding students in the
Bachelor's programs and Continuing Education Bachelor's programs of
National Taipei University (hereinafter referred to as "the University") to
continue their studies in the relevant Master's programs of the University,
with the aim of achieving continuous learning and shortening the duration
of study.
Article 2 Students in the Bachelor's programs and Continuing Education Bachelor's
programs who are in their third year or above and have studied at the
University for at least two full semesters with outstanding performance may
apply to the relevant Master's programs before the start of each semester.
The admission quota, definition of relevant departments/institutes, and
screening regulations shall be established by each department/institute.
Article 3 Admitted students shall possess the dual status of a Bachelor's degree
candidate and a Master's program preparatory graduate student (hereinafter
referred to as "Preparatory Student").
Article 4 After obtaining the qualification of a Preparatory Student, students must
obtain their Bachelor's degree by the end of the eighth semester (inclusive)
and participate in the University's Master's program recommendation
screening or general admission examination. Only after being admitted and
registering shall they formally acquire the status of a Master's program
graduate student.
Article 5 The quota for Preparatory Students formally admitted to the Master's
program shall be included in the Master's program admission quota for that
academic year of the respective department/institute.
Article 6 After Preparatory Students acquire the status of Master's program graduate
students, the Master's level courses taken during their university study
period with a grade of seventy (70) or above may be applied for credit
### Page 2
waiver against the required credits of the Master's program (excluding
thesis credits) in accordance with the National Taipei University
Regulations for Student Credit Waiver. However, if the Master's level
courses have already been counted towards the graduation credits of the
Bachelor's program or Continuing Education Bachelor's program, they may
not be applied for a waiver of Master's program credits.
Students must fulfill the graduation requirements of their original Bachelor's degree
department and the Master's degree requirements of the department/institute
they intend to study in to be issued both Bachelor's and Master's degree
certificates.
Article 7 Matters not provided for in these Regulations shall be handled in accordance
with relevant regulations of the University and the Ministry of Education.
Article 8 These Regulations shall be implemented after being approved by the
Academic Affairs Meeting and ratified by the President; the same applies to
amendments.

## National Taipei University Enforcement Rules for Master’s and Doctoral Degree Examinations (Rev. Oct. 2021 )
### Page 1
National Taipei University Enforcement Rules for Master’s and Doctoral Degree
Examinations
In case of any discrepancy or inconsistency between the English and Chinese versions of the
regulations, the Chinese version shall take precedence.
Last Revised: Jan. 2022
Article 1 These Rules were established in accordance with the Degree Conferral Act, Article 9 of
the National Taipei University Regulations for Master’s and Doctoral Programs, and
related laws and regulations.
Article 2 Whether a master’s or doctoral thesis/dissertation may be substituted by works or proof of
achievement along with written reports, technical reports, or professional practice reports,
shall be determined in accordance with the National Taipei University Academic
Regulations and the Regulations Governing Determination of Procedures for Naming
Degrees, Degree Conferral Criteria and Alternatives to Submission of a Master's Degree
Thesis or Doctoral Dissertation of the Ministry of Education.
Article 2-1 Master's degree examinations are conducted by the Examination Committee organized
by each department (institute). The Examination Committee shall have 3-5 members
appointed by the president at the request of the department (institute) chairperson. The
thesis advisor may not serve as the convener. Examination committee members must
specialize in the research field of the master’s degree student and have one of the
following qualifications:
I. Current or former professor, associate professor, or assistant
professor.
II. Academician of the Academia Sinica or current or former research
fellow, associate research fellow, or assistant research fellow of the
Academia Sinica.
III. Holds a doctoral degree and has had significant academic
accomplishments.
1
### Page 2
Specializes in a rare or distinctive subject field or professional practice,
and has had significant academic or professional accomplishments.
Determination standards for Subparagraphs 3 and 4 in the preceding paragraph shall be
established by the department, institute, college, or program affairs meeting responsible
for conferring the degree.
The Doctoral Degree Examination Committee shall consist of 5-9 members
recommended by the dissertation advisor from both inside and outside the University. At
least one third (inclusive) of the committee members shall be from outside the University.
The dissertation advisor is an ex officio member and may not serve as the convener. The
list of committee members shall be discussed in a department (institute) affairs meeting
and submitted by the department (institute) chairperson to the president, who shall
appoint the members and establish the Doctoral Degree Examination Committee.
Examination committee members must specialize in the doctoral candidate’s research
field and have one of the following qualifications:
I. Current or former professor or associate professor.
II. Academician of the Academia Sinica or current or former research fellow or
associate research fellow of the Academia Sinica.
III. Holds a doctoral degree and has had significant academic accomplishments.
IV. Specializes in a rare or distinctive subject field and has had significant academic or
professional accomplishments.
Determination standards for Subparagraphs 3 and 4 in the preceding paragraph shall
be prescribed by the department, institute, college, or program affairs meeting
conferring the degree.
If the examination committee member selected for the student is the student or their
spouse, former spouse, blood relative within the fourth degree of kinship, or current
or previous relative by marriage within the third degree of kinship, the examination
committee member must recuse themselves.
Article 3 A passing grade for degree examinations is 70 points out of 100 points. The date of degree
2
### Page 3
examinations each semester shall be based on the academic calendar of National Taipei
University.
Article 4 After the thesis and abstract have been approved by the master’s student’s thesis advisor
and are in compliance with the relevant provisions of the National Taipei University
Regulations for the Implementation of Academic Research Ethics Education, the student is
eligible to apply to take the degree examination.
After passing the doctoral candidate qualifying examination and in compliance with the
relevant provisions of the National Taipei University Regulations for the Implementation
of Academic Research Ethics Education, doctoral students are eligible to apply for the
degree examination.
The thesis title and content of master’s and doctoral students must be approved by their
thesis advisor and aligned with the student’s department (institute, degree programs) and
the respective field of expertise before they are permitted to apply for the degree
examination.
The students who are in preparation for thesis degree examinations are required to
complete a ‘thesis originality check’ which will be provided to the members of the degree
examination committee for reference. The criteria for the originality check are
determined by each department(institute, degree program) and college.
Master's and doctoral students applying for a thesis examination must complete the
application process in the specified format one month before the date of the degree
examination. After approved by the advising professor and department (institute) head, an
official appointment letter issued in the University’s name will be sent by the department
(institute) to the examination committee members. The date and location of the thesis
examination will be determined by the department (institute), and shall be announced or
communicated to the candidates one week in advance of the examination date by the
department (institute).
Article 5 The thesis/dissertation (including abstract) of master's and doctoral students shall be
written in Chinese in principle. Theses/Dissertations written in foreign languages must
3
### Page 4
include a Chinese and foreign language abstract. The thesis/dissertations that have been
previously awarded other academic degrees may not be submitted. The thesis/dissertation
(including abstract) shall be delivered by the department (institute) to examination
committee members before the examination for review.
Article 6 Guests should be permitted to attend the oral examination of master's and doctoral
students. The examination committee convener shall appoint a designated person to
diligently records the proceedings, and the records shall be submitted to the department
(institute) for future reference. Guests must leave the premises when oral examination
committee members are assessing scores. The degree examination score results notice
shall be forwarded to the Academic Affairs Office for future reference.
Article 7 the degree examinations scores shall be assessed by the committee members in
attendance through anonymous voting, with each member allowed to vote only once. The
convener shall then calculate the average score based on the evaluations of all
examination committee members. This calculated score will be considered as the
student's score result for the degree examination. The degree examination score notice
shall be signed by the committee members and the department (institute) chairperson.
If half or more (inclusive) of committee members give a score under 70 points for the
master's degree examination, the student will be deemed as having failed the examination,
regardless of the score given by other committee members, and the average score will not
be calculated. All members of the committee are required to attend the examination
committee meeting personally, and may not appoint proxies. Master's degree
examinations must have at least two thirds (inclusive) or more of committee members
and at least three members in attendance.
If one third or more of committee members give a score under 70 points, the student will
be deemed as having failed the doctoral degree examination, regardless of the score given
by other committee members, and the average score will not be calculated. All members
of the committee are required to attend the examination committee meeting personally,
and may not appoint proxies. Degree examinations must have at least two thirds
4
### Page 5
(inclusive) or more of committee members and at least five members in attendance. At
least one third (inclusive) of the members in attendance must be external members for the
degree examination to be held.
Article 8 Students who failed the degree examination but have not exceeded the period of study
may apply to retake the examination once only in the following academic year or
semester. Students who fail the re-examination shall be withdrawn from the University.
Article 9 A make-up test score of 70 points or higher for degree examinations will be recorded as 70
points.
Article 10 For master's and doctoral students, the graduation grade is calculated as the average of
their academic average and their degree examination grade.
Article 11 If the thesis/dissertation were determined by the Degree Examination Committee to be
counterfeited, altered, plagiarized, written by someone other than the student themselves,
or involved in any other form of fraudulent action, the student will be deemed to have
failed the examination. In such cases, the student’s degree will be revoked, and an
announcement will be issued to cancel any previously issued degree certificates. The
student will be notified to return their degree certificate. Other junior colleges,
universities, and relevant institutions will be informed about the degree revocation and
the cancellation of the degree certificate. The student shall be required to withdraw from
studies as a punishment.
Article 12 After passing the degree examination, the result notification letter must be submitted to
the Academic Affairs Office before the end of the current semester. The
thesis/dissertation must be submitted before the start of next semester. Students who fail
to do so will be required to enroll in the following semester and will be considered as
graduating in the next semester.
Article 13 Master's and doctoral students are required to submit two hard copies of their
thesis/dissertation, signed by their thesis advisor and members of the thesis examination
committee for departmental (institute, degree program, college) review. Before apply for
departure from the university, students must also distribute the hard copies of their
5
### Page 6
thesis/dissertation to both the university library and their respective department (institute,
degree program, college). Each department (institute, degree program, college) should
forward these copies to the Academic Affairs Office before the next semester's
registration, in accordance with the specified regulations. Any additional requirements
regarding the number of copies to be archived by departments (institutes, degree
programs, colleges) should be followed if such requirements exist. The format for
thesis/dissertation writing will be specified separately.
Master’s and doctoral students must adhere to the requirement outlined in the previous
paragraph, which mandates the submission of both paper copies and electronic version
(including relevant bibliographic information) of their thesis/dissertation to our
university’s Electronic Theses & Dissertations Service System (ETDS). These paper
copies, in conjunction with the electronic versions, will be sent by the university to the
National Library for preservation.
The thesis/dissertation submitted to the National Library and the university library for
preservation, including the works and achievements mentioned in Article 2, along with
written reports, technical reports, or professional practice reports should be made
accessible to the public as a fundamental principle. They may be available for public
viewing in print within the library or accessed electronically through designated
equipment. However, after a review by the thesis advisor and the department (institute,
degree program, college) head, if it is determined that the thesis/dissertation meets the
conditions stipulated in Article 16 of the Degree Granting Act (including cases involving
confidentiality, patent matters, or situations where it is legally prohibited to disclose), an
application may be made for a limited period of non-disclosure (hereinafter referred to as
"postponed disclosure"). Still, perpetual non-disclosure is not allowed.
To apply for delayed disclosure, a formal application form must be completed.
Additionally, supporting documents that align with the reasons for the application should
be provided. The application should be personally signed by the thesis advisor and the
department (institute, degree program, college) head, and bear the official departmental
6
### Page 7
seal. The application should then be submitted to the library for processing.
The application for delayed disclosure is limited to a single request. If a student does not
request delayed disclosure at the time of submitting their thesis/dissertation, it will be
considered as their agreement for immediate disclosure, and no further requests to change
the delayed disclosure period will be accepted.
To ensure both the quality control mechanism for degree theses and academic ethics, once
a degree thesis has been assessed by the examination committee and submitted to the
library for preservation, and its evaluation outcome is finalized, no exchanges or
replacements will be allowed.
To promote academic dissemination, the preservation and provision of this degree thesis
are authorized in accordance with copyright laws for reproduction, open transmission
within or outside the library via the internet, or other activities that involve copyright, for
the purpose of non-profit academic circulation and sharing.
Article 14 These Rules were approved during a meeting of the Academic Affairs Conference,
submitted to the Ministry of Education for future reference, and shall take effect from the
date of announcement. The same applies to any subsequent revisions.
7

## National Taipei University Regulations For Master’s And Doctoral Programs (Rev. Oct. 2019)
### Page 1
National Taipei University Regulations For Master’s And Doctoral Programs
In case of any discrepancy or inconsistency between the English and Chinese versions of the
regulations, the Chinese version shall take precedence.
Last Revised: Oct. 2019
Article 1 These Regulations are formulated in accordance with the University Act and its Enforcement
Rules, the Degree Conferral Act, and relevant laws and regulations.
Article 2 Applicants who have obtained a bachelor’s degree from a university accredited by the Ministry
of Education, or from a foreign university recognized under the Ministry of Education’s
Regulations Governing the Accreditation of Foreign Academic Credentials, or who possess
equivalent academic qualifications, and who have passed the entrance examination or screening
for the University’s master’s programs; foreign students approved by the Ministry of Education
who have passed the University’s screening process; foreign students admitted in accordance with
the University’s Regulations Governing Admission of International Students; or overseas Chinese
students admitted in accordance with the University’s Regulations Governing Admission of
Overseas Chinese Students, may enroll in the University’s master’s programs.
Applicants who have obtained a master’s degree from a university accredited by the Ministry of
Education, or from a foreign university recognized under the Ministry of Education’s Regulations
Governing the Accreditation of Foreign Academic Credentials, or who possess equivalent
academic qualifications, and who have passed the entrance examination or screening for the
University’s doctoral programs; students approved to proceed directly to doctoral studies in
accordance with the University’s Regulations for Direct Admission to Doctoral Programs; foreign
students approved by the Ministry of Education who have passed the University’s screening
process; foreign students admitted in accordance with the University’s Regulations Governing
Admission of International Students; or overseas Chinese students admitted in accordance with
the University’s Regulations Governing Admission of Overseas Chinese Students, may enroll in
the University’s doctoral programs.
Details regarding admissions to master’s and doctoral programs shall be prescribed separately.
Article 3 Master’s students, beginning in the second semester of their first academic year, may, with the
approval of the department (institute) chair, select a thesis advisor and determine a thesis topic.
However, master’s students who, during their undergraduate studies, have completed more than
one-half of the graduation credits required for the master’s program by their department (institute)
may, beginning in the first semester of their first academic year after admission, select a thesis
advisor and determine a thesis topic with the approval of the department (institute) chair.
Doctoral students, upon admission, shall, with the approval of the department (institute) chair,
select one or more thesis advisors, who shall be responsible for supervising the doctoral
dissertation.
### Page 2
If a selected thesis advisor is the student themself, or the student’s spouse, former spouse, blood
relative within the fourth degree, or relative by marriage within the third degree, or has previously
had such a relationship, the advisor shall recuse themself from serving in that supervisory role.
Whether a master’s or doctoral thesis may be substituted with creative works, certificates of
achievement accompanied by a written report, technical reports, or professional practice reports
shall be handled in accordance with the University’s Academic Regulations and the Ministry of
Education’s Regulations Governing Degree Titles, Degree Conferral Requirements, and Criteria
for Substitution of Master’s and Doctoral Theses.
Article 4 Courses taken by master’s and doctoral students shall be implemented only after approval by the
relevant levels of curriculum committees and the Academic Affairs Meeting.
Article 5 Master’s and doctoral students who are required to make up undergraduate-level foundational
courses shall do so as determined by the department (institute) chair and the thesis advisor.
Credits earned from such make-up courses shall not be included in the semester grade point
average or graduation credit requirements. Students who have not completed the required make-
up courses may not apply for the degree examination.
Master’s and doctoral students who are required to retake or make up military training courses for
reserve officer selection purposes may apply to do so. Credits earned from such courses shall not
be included in the semester grade point average or graduation credit requirements. Students who
have not completed such courses or whose grades do not meet the preliminary reserve officer
selection standards may not participate in the secondary selection examination.
Article 6 Master’s students shall complete at least 24 credits, excluding thesis credits, which shall be
counted separately. Doctoral students shall complete at least 18 credits. Students admitted directly
to doctoral programs shall complete at least 30 credits, including up to 12 credits transferred from
previously completed master’s-level coursework. Thesis credits shall be counted separately.
If a department (institute) requires a higher number of graduation credits, such requirements shall
be prescribed by the department (institute) affairs meeting and implemented after approval by the
University.
Article 7 The duration of study for master’s programs shall range from one to four years. In-service students
who fail to complete required coursework or the thesis within the prescribed period may apply for
a one-year extension. The duration of study for doctoral programs shall range from two to seven
years, with the same extension provisions applicable to in-service students.
Students who are pregnant, have given birth, or are raising children under three years of age may
apply for extensions on a case-by-case basis.
Students holding a disability certificate or recognized as students with special education needs by
the Ministry of Education may be granted an additional extension of up to three years.
### Page 3
Article 8 A score of 70 shall be considered a passing grade for master’s and doctoral students, with 100
being the maximum score. With approval by the department (institute) affairs meeting, course
grades may be assessed on a “Pass/Fail” basis. Required courses that are failed or not passed must
be retaken.
Article 9 Degree examinations for master’s and doctoral programs shall, in principle, be conducted orally
and held periodically by the University. Where necessary, departments (institutes) may also
conduct written examinations.
Detailed regulations governing degree examinations shall be prescribed separately, reported to the
Ministry of Education for recordation, and implemented from the date of promulgation. The same
shall apply to any amendments.
Article 10 (For students admitted before and including Academic Year 2004) Master’s and doctoral students
shall be dismissed if any of the following applies: I. Master’s and in-service master’s students fail
credits amounting to one-half of the total credits taken in a semester, on two cumulative
occasions. II. The maximum period of study expires without completion of required courses and
credits. III. Doctoral candidates fail the qualifying examination and fail the re-examination. IV.
The degree examination is failed and the student is not eligible for re-examination, or the re-
examination is failed.
Article 10 (For students admitted from Academic Year 2005 onward) Master’s and doctoral students shall
be dismissed if any of the following applies: I. Master’s and in-service master’s students fail
credits amounting to one-half of the total credits taken in a semester, on two cumulative
occasions. II. Upon expiration of the maximum period of study, the student fails to meet the
requirements prescribed in Article 72, Paragraph 1 of the University Academic Regulations. III.
Except as otherwise provided by departments (institutes), doctoral students fail the subject
examination after one re-examination. IV. Doctoral candidates fail the qualifying examination
after one re-examination. V. The degree examination is failed and the student is not eligible for re-
examination, or, if eligible, fails the re-examination.
Article 10-1 Overseas Chinese students, international students, Mongolian and Tibetan students returning
from overseas for further studies, indigenous students, students holding disability certificates, and
children of overseas-assigned personnel in master’s and in-service master’s programs shall be
dismissed if the number of failed credits in a semester reaches two-thirds of the total credits taken
in that semester, on two cumulative occasions.
Article 10-2 Master’s and in-service master’s students who take no more than nine credits in a semester shall
not be subject to the restrictions set forth in Article 10 and Article 10-1.
Article 11 Master’s students who have completed the prescribed period of study, fulfilled required
coursework and credits, and submitted a thesis that passes the examination by the master’s degree
examination committee shall be awarded the master’s degree.
### Page 4
Doctoral students who have completed the prescribed period of study, fulfilled required
coursework and credits, and passed the doctoral qualifying examination shall become doctoral
candidates and may apply for the doctoral degree examination. Upon passing the degree
examination, the doctoral degree shall be conferred by the University. Regulations governing
doctoral qualifying examinations shall be prescribed separately, reported to the Ministry of
Education for recordation, and implemented from the date of promulgation. The same shall apply
to any amendments.
Article 12 Except under special circumstances approved by both the original and prospective departments
(institutes), with the consent of the Dean of Academic Affairs and approval by the President,
master’s and doctoral students may not apply to transfer departments (institutes).
Students who withdraw before completing one semester of study shall not be issued any
certification documents by the University.
Article 13 Master’s and doctoral students shall comply with all University regulations. Any violations shall
be handled in accordance with the University Academic Regulations.
Article 14 Matters not provided for in these Regulations shall be handled in accordance with the University
Act and its Enforcement Rules, the Degree Conferral Act, the University Academic Regulations,
and relevant laws and regulations.
Article 15 These Regulations shall be implemented from the date of announcement after being passed by the
Academic Affairs Meeting and reported to the Ministry of Education for reference; the same
applies to amendments.

## National Taipei University Regulations for Implementation of Distance Education. (Rev. Oct. 2024)
### Page 1
National Taipei University Regulations for Implementation of Distance Education
In case of any discrepancy or inconsistency between the English and Chinese versions of the
regulations, the Chinese version shall take precedence.
Last Revised: Oct. 2024
Article 1. The National Taipei University (NTPU or “the University”) Regulations for the
Implementation of Distance Education (the “Regulations”) are formulated in accordance
with the Ministry of Education’s Implementation Regulations Regarding Distance
Learning in the University.
Article 2. “ Distance education” refers to teaching and learning activities conducted between
lecturers and students over telecommunications networks, computer networks, video-on-
demand (VoD) channels, and other media.
Distance education may be conducted in synchronous (live video), asynchronous (online),
and blended formats. It should be implemented through a learning management system
that supports teaching implementation, records students’ learning activities, and provides
other learning support functions in compliance with the teaching standards of the
University.
The distance education referred to in the Regulations shall mean courses in which 50%
or more of the teaching hours are conducted via distance education.
The blended format distance education referred to in the Regulations includes distance
education courses that offer both synchronous and asynchronous instruction, with at least
50% of the teaching hours conducted synchronously (via live video) or in-person.
Article 3. For courses implementing distance education for the first time, or for courses that have
previously utilized distance education but wish to revise the course plan, a “course
planning application” and “Distance Education Course Quality Assessment Form” must
be submitted by the respective department (degree program, institute, section, or center)
one semester prior to offering the course. The course may only be offered after it has been
reviewed and approved by the three-tier curriculum committees (departmental, college,
and university levels). To ensure the quality of distance education, a “Distance Education
Continuation Form” shall be approved by the curriculum committee of the respective
department (degree programs, institutes, offices, centers) one semester prior to the new
### Page 2
semester and submitted to the curriculum committee for future reference.
The course plan shall clearly state course objectives, target students, course syllabus,
methods of instruction, teacher-student interaction/discussions, assignment submission,
performance assessment, and other course notices.
Article 4. Graduating students may not select distance education courses exceeding half of the total
credits required for graduation. Additionally, except for extension students, the limit for
selecting distance education courses each semester cannot exceed half of the total credits.
For students whose distance education credits exceed one-third but are less than half of
the total credits required for graduation, the University must submit the relevant
regulations on the offering and quality assurance of distance education courses to the
Ministry of Education for review and approval before these courses can be offered.
For students who apply for credit exemption based on credits obtained through distance
education in continuing education, if these credits exceed one-third of the total credits
required for graduation, the University must register and report these courses to the
Ministry of Education for future reference.
The course selection and credit recognition regulations for students enrolled in courses
offered by the Ministry of Education’s “Taiwan Artificial Intelligence College Alliance”
(TAICA) shall be handled in accordance with the “National Taipei University AI Program
Alliance Course Selection Guidelines.”
Article 5. Students who wish to enroll in distance education courses offered by the University or
other universities must register during the course selection period, following the
University's course selection procedures. The class schedule will be determined by the
host university.
Article 6. Lecturers conducting distance education courses must adhere to the credit calculation
regulations outlined in the University Act Implementation Rules. The course objectives,
teaching materials, records of teacher-student interactions/discussions, performance
assessments, attendance records, and assignment submissions shall be uploaded to the
University’s E-learning platform after the course concludes. These records shall be
retained for at least five years for future grade inquiries, teaching evaluations, or
### Page 3
inspections.
Article 7. Lecturers are required to upload teaching materials to the University’s e-learning platform
or the platforms accredited by the Ministry of Education when conducting distance
education courses. These platforms shall record course content, teaching progress,
performance assessments, teacher-student interactions/discussions, assignment
submissions, and provide a system usage manual. Additionally, lecturers must establish
online discussion forums or provide email accounts and other contact channels to
facilitate two-way communication related to the course.
Article 7.1 Lecturers are required to complete the “Distance Education Self-evaluation Report” within
one month of the course’s conclusion. The course-offering unit shall have the report
reviewed by the respective curriculum committee in the following semester and submit it
to the Office of Academic Affairs for future reference.
Distance education courses shall be subject to teaching evaluation in accordance with the
University’s standard teaching evaluation procedures. If a course receives an end-of-term
teaching satisfaction score below 3.5, the lecturer must submit a “Distance Education
Teaching Improvement Plan.” The course may continue to be offered only after the plan
has been reviewed and approved in accordance with the continuation procedures outlined
in Article 3, Paragraph 1.
The aforementioned self-evaluation report and teaching improvement plan shall serve as
references for course evaluation and continuation. They shall be retained for at least five
years for future evaluations and inspections conducted by the Ministry of Education.
Article 8. In the event of a network disruption or other factors that result in the suspension of a
synchronous distance education course, the lecturer shall either send a recording of the
suspended session to the receiving educational institution or schedule a make-up session.
Article 9. Depending on course requirements, lecturers may assess students’ performance through
mid-term and final examinations, reports, assignments, and periodic tests. Mid-term and
final examinations shall take place in a classroom and will be arranged directly by the
instructors through the venue reservation system.
Article 10. Lecturers and students must not use the courses for any purposes other than teaching and
### Page 4
interaction, nor engage in any illegal activities during their participation in distance
education courses. Compliance with intellectual property rights regulations is mandatory.
Any criminal or infringing actions will be addressed in accordance with relevant laws and
regulations.
Article 11. For asynchronous (online) distance education courses, the teaching hours for lecturers
who have registered for the Massive Open Online Courses (MOOCs) program shall
adhere to the regulations outlined in the MOOCs operation guidelines. However, for
asynchronous distance education courses that have not been registered as MOOCs, the
teaching hours for lecturers will be calculated on a six-week basis. The aforementioned
teaching hours are still subject to the regulations stipulated in the University's
“Regulations for the Calculation of Faculty Members’ Teaching Hours and Overtime
Payment.”
Each lecturer may only apply to teach one distance education course per semester
(synchronous or synchronous and asynchronous blended formats). The number of
asynchronous distance education courses that may be applied for each semester shall be
handled in accordance with the regulations outlined in the University’s MOOCs
operational guidelines.
Article 12. The aforementioned distance education course hours shall include lectures, teacher-
student interactions and discussions, examinations, and other learning activities.
Article 13. Any other matters not covered herein shall be handled in accordance with the University
Academic Regulations and relevant regulations.
Article 14. These Regulations shall be implemented after approval by the Academic Affairs
Conference and subsequent endorsement by the President. The same applies to any
subsequent revisions.

## National Taipei University Regulations for Faculty Taking Leave, Employment of Substitute Teachers, and Making up Classes.(Rev. Oct. 2024)
### Page 1
National Taipei University Regulations for Faculty Taking Leave,
Employment of Substitute Teachers, and Making up Classes
In case of any discrepancy or inconsistency between the English and Chinese versions of the
regulations, the Chinese version shall take precedence.
Last Revised: Oct. 2024
Article 1. The National Taipei University (hereinafter referred to as the University or NTPU)
Regulations for Faculty Taking Leave, Substitute Teaching, and Making up Classes
(hereinafter referred to as the Regulations) are formulated in accordance with Article 2 of
the Regulations for Rescheduling, Making up, and Substituting Missed Classes Due to
Faculty Taking Leave in National Universities and Colleges.
Article 2. Faculty members of the University who require leave for personal reasons (including
personal matters, illness, marriage, bereavement, maternity leave, official duties, or official
international travel) must notify the Academic Affairs Office, Curriculum Affairs Division,
at least one day prior to the scheduled class to facilitate public announcement.
Article 3. The substitute teachers of our institution shall be subject to the following conditions, unless
otherwise specified by these regulations or other laws:
I. Faculty taking leave:
(I). Taking maternity leave, miscarriage leave, prenatal examination leave, and
paternity leave.
(II). Taking 7 consecutive days or more of personal leave and family care leave.
(III). Taking 14 consecutive days of marriage leave.
(IV).Taking 15 consecutive days of bereavement leave.
(V). Taking 21 consecutive days or more of sick leave or official business trips (leave).
II. Faculty members having an interim absence for some reason (including resigning
without giving prior notice and failing in faculty employment qualification).
III. Faculty members taking leave without pay, or suspension pending investigation.
Regarding the previous subparagraph, faculty members should comply with personnel-
related regulations.
The faculty members of the University who request to take leave should make up their
classes and provide an explanation on the leave application form, except for the cases that
### Page 2
meet the conditions specified in Article 3 allowing for a substitute teacher.
Article 4. After a teacher takes leave, the remaining classes should be priority covered by full-time
faculty members with insufficient teaching hours.
If the total teaching hours of full-time faculty members, including substitute teaching hours,
fail to meet the minimum required teaching hours for the academic year, they will not
qualify for substitute teaching payment. The maximum allowable excess teaching hours
after incorporating substitute teaching is four hours per week per semester.
Adjunct faculty members are limited to a maximum of four teaching hours per week per
semester after combining substitute teaching hours. However, if there are specific
regulations governing the calculation of teaching hours and excess hourly payments for
faculty at the university, those regulations shall take precedence.
Article 5. If the departments, institutes, and centers of the university have instructional needs and
meet the criteria specified in Article 3, they are permitted to hire substitute teachers. The
qualifications for their appointment shall follow the provisions outlined in the “National
Taipei University Regulations for Faculty Appointment and Promotion Review” regarding
adjunct faculty members appointments, and “National Taipei University Regulations for
Doctoral Students Serving as Adjunct Lectures for Teaching”.
The new substitute teachers are required to prepare a comprehensive resume along with
relevant academic documents prior to the commencement of classes. These materials will
be submitted to the respective department, institute, office, or center for review by the
Academic Affairs Committee. Upon approval, the personnel office will determine their
rank, and the teaching hour limit of maximum four hours per week.
Article 6. The hourly wage for substitute teachers is determined based on their ranks.
The standard hourly pay for substitute teachers should accord with the standard hourly pay
of adjunct faculty at different ranks and should be given on the basis of the actual teaching
hours.
The class size for courses conducted by substitute teachers who are not full-time faculty
members of the university should adhere to the standards outlined in Article 3 of the
“National Taipei University Principles for Course Offerings.” However, courses for
### Page 3
substitute teaching requested by faculty members after the significant add/drop period are
exempt from this restriction, as stipulated in Article 3, Section 1.
Article 7. As a full-time faculty member is on leave and the hourly pay of a substitute teacher is
covered by the university, the overtime pay during their leave should be deducted
accordingly. When an adjunct faculty member is on leave, the hourly pay for a substitute
teacher shall be handled in accordance with Article 17 of the “Regulations for the
Employment of Adjunct Faculty Members in Tertiary Education Institutions.”
Article 8. If an adjunct faculty member takes leave due to unforeseen circumstances at the start of the
next semester, they shall be suspended from their appointment, and the course will be
assigned to another faculty member.
Article 9. When the faculty member's reason for leave ends, they should promptly notify the
Academic Affairs Office to resume teaching duties and cease payment to the substitute
instructor. When the reason for substitution ends, the substitute instructor should be
promptly relieved of their duties, without any grounds to request an extension.
Article 10. These Regulations shall be implemented after approval by the Academic Affairs
Conference and subsequent endorsement by the President. The same applies to any
subsequent revisions.

## National Taipei University Regulations for Offering Summer Session Programs (Rev. Oct. 2023)
### Page 1
National Taipei University Regulations for Offering Summer Session Programs
In case of any discrepancy or inconsistency between the English and Chinese versions of the
regulations, the Chinese version shall take precedence.
Last Revised: Oct. 2023
Article 1. The National Taipei University (NTPU or “the University”) formulates the National Taipei
University Regulations for Offering Summer Session Programs (the “Program”) in
accordance with Article 23 of the National Taipei University Academic Regulations.
Article 2. The University may offer summer session programs as needed (hereinafter referred to as
“the Programs”). A maximum of two summer session programs can be held per summer,
with each session generally lasting six weeks. One credit may be issued for 18 hours of
instruction.
The duration of summer session programs shall not be reduced by employing intensive
teaching methods. However, courses offered under Article 3, Paragraph 1, Subparagraph 6
of these Regulations are exempt from this provision.
Students who are enrolled in summer session programs are not permitted to request early
commencement for any reasons, including military service or admission to graduate
programs.
Article 3. Students may apply for the establishment of courses in a summer session program under
one of the following circumstances:
I. The student failed a required course and needs to retake the course.
II. Transfer students are required to complete courses in the departmental curriculum
designated for earlier years.
III. Students who are enrolled in a minor study program or a double major program should
complete the required courses.
IV. The graduating students who lack sufficient credits (including elective credits), or who
do not meet other department-specific graduation requirements, are required to
complete or repeat a course to graduate from the university.
V. Master’s and doctoral students who need to retake basic courses or earn education
credits required by students in an education program.
VI. Students who fail to meet the English proficiency graduation requirements set by the
### Page 2
university are required to complete the “English Enhancement” course in order to
qualify for graduation requirements.
VII. Any other courses that are deemed suitable for the summer session program, which
have been approved by the department or institute after review.
The same subject is allowed to be offered one class during the summer session program,
except for language general education, integrated and university wide language courses.
Article 4. Students are not eligible to apply for the summer session program if they meet any of the
following conditions:
I. The student has not completed the make-up examination for subjects they were absent
in the final exams.
II. During the leave of absence.
Article 5. The courses may only be offered when the income from credit fees reaches the instructors’
hourly pay standard. However, a course may also be offered if selected by an overseas
Chinese student and meets the subsidy standards outlined in the Ministry of Education’s
Implementation Directions for Subsidizing Senior Secondary Schools or Above to Provide
Tutorials for Overseas Compatriot Students.
Courses that do not meet the criteria in the preceding paragraph may only be offered after
obtaining approval through a special request process.
Article 6. Bachelor’s degree programs and Extension Education Bachelor Programs may offer
summer session programs, and students are allowed to select courses across these programs
without being subject to Articles 2 and 3 of the “National Taipei University Regulations for
Course Registration between Undergraduate Programs and Extension Education Bachelor
Programs.” However, a course will only be offered if it is selected by students from its
original program.
Article 6.1 Non-NTPU students who wish to take a summer session program offered by the university
should obtain approval from their home university.
Students who wish to enroll in summer session programs at other universities must first
submit a summer session program application to NTPU. They may only apply if the
course is not offered at NTPU, if the course at NTPU has not reached the minimum
### Page 3
enrollment, or if there are no equivalent courses available. Additionally, they must
obtain approval from their respective departments (institutes) before proceeding with
the application to enroll in summer session programs at other universities.
Article 6.2 Bachelor’s degree programs and the Extension Education Bachelor Program may open
courses to college-bound students, or specialized classes with foundational subjects for
college-bound students may be offered. However, these specialized classes may only be
offered if there are prospective university students enrolled in the bachelor’s degree
programs or the Extension Education Bachelor Program at the University.
College-bound students are defined as high school students who have received an
official proof of admission from a college.
Article 7. Students who take summer session programs offered by the University or other universities
may take a maximum of 9 credits in each term.
Article 8. The registration deadline for the Programs shall be specified on the University’s calendar,
and students shall personally register on the specified date. Late registrations will not be
accepted for any reason.
Article 9. Students may request a refund only if the Program is canceled due to insufficient
enrollment. No refunds will be granted for other programs once enrollment and payment
have been completed.
Article 10. The final exams of the Programs shall be held at the end of each term. Students are not
allowed to take leave unless they provide a medical certificate from a public hospital for
severe illness. For approved leave, the course instructor shall arrange a makeup exam
for the student.
Article 11. The students enrolled in the Programs shall pay credit fees in accordance with the
University's tuition fee standards. For courses that include internship components, an
additional internship fee shall be paid (the internship fee is half of the credit fee for every
18 hours).
Article 12. The approved courses for the Program shall be offered by the relevant departments, which
shall appoint the instructors. Each department may determine, based on actual
circumstances, whether to offer its specialized courses and appoint instructors
### Page 4
accordingly.
The hourly pay for the Program instructors shall be according to the standard rates for
part-time faculty members.
When bachelor program courses meet the Article 5, Paragraph 1, the course instructor
may choose to receive hourly pay for teaching summer session program or, with
approval from the department (institute) director to count the hours towards the basic
teaching hours in the following academic year. However, they are not permitted to
collect extended hours pay in the following academic year, and the number of courses
is limited to a maximum of two courses, each offering 6 credits per academic year.
Course instructors who choose to receive hourly pay for summer session program may
have their pay calculated based on weighted teaching hours if they meet the conditions
set forth in Article 7, Subparagraph 1 of the National Taipei University Regulations for
Calculation of Teaching Hours and Extended Hours Pay.
Article 13. Students in the Summer Session Program shall be evaluated for their academic
performance as follows:
I. Academic performance for both passed and failed courses shall be recorded on the
academic transcript.
II. The credits earned from the summer session program shall not be combined with
semester credits, and individual course grades shall not be averaged with semester
grades. However, both the credits and grades shall be calculated in their graduation
grade.
Article 14. The hourly pay expenses of each course shall not exceed the total amount of income from
the credit fees.
Article 15. These Regulations shall be implemented after approval by the Academic Affairs
Conference and subsequent endorsement by the President. The same applies to any
subsequent revisions.

## National Taipei University Regulations for Course Registration between Undergraduate Programs and Extension Education Bachelor Programs (Rev. Mar. 2024)
### Page 1
National Taipei University Regulations for Course Registration between
Undergraduate Programs and Extension Education Bachelor Programs
In case of any discrepancy or inconsistency between the English and Chinese versions of the
regulations, the Chinese version shall take precedence.
Last Revised: March 2024
Article 1 The Regulations are formulated in accordance with Article 21 of the National Taipei
University (NTPU or “the University”) Academic Regulations and Article 10 of the
University Regulations for Course Selection.
Article 2 Students in Undergraduate Programs and Extension Education Bachelor Programs
may not take courses in each other’s program. This rule does not apply under the
following circumstances:
1. Dimensional general education courses.
2. Graduating students who need to retake or repeat the courses.
3. Students may request enrollment in interdisciplinary required courses by
providing reasonable justifications and obtaining approval from the course
instructor, the department offering the course, and the director of the student’s
respective department. For students in the Extension Education Bachelor Program
pursuing Pre-service Teacher Education courses, approval from the Teacher
Education Center is required. However, enrollment in the same course during the
same time slot is limited to courses with available seats. Any additional
regulations specified by the offering department shall apply.
Undergraduate students and Extension Education Bachelor Program students may
take elective courses in each other’s programs.
Article 3 Courses taken under the aforementioned conditions shall not exceed 1/3 of the total
course credits in the current semester, except for students who have extended their
