study period or Extension Education Bachelor Program students taking professional
education courses offered by the Center for Teacher Education. Extension
Education Program students whose average grades in the previous semester rank
among the top 20% of the department may exceed 1/2 of the total course credits in
the current semester, and this provision shall not be subject to the regulations
outlined in Article 2 of these regulations.
Article 4 Students under the aforementioned conditions shall complete course selection
online before the deadlines specified by both programs and obtain approval from
### Page 2
the department offering the course.
Article 5 The number of students allowed from the other program shall be determined by each
department. However, graduating students who have course schedule conflicts due
to retaking or repeating a course and are unable to resolve them may submit a
separate request. After obtaining approval from their respective department and the
department offering the course, they will not be subject to number limit.
The courses can only be offered if they are selected by students from the original
undergraduate programs or the extension education bachelor programs.
Article 6 Course fees for undergraduate programs students taking extension education
bachelor programs are waived; extension education bachelor programs students
taking undergraduate program courses will be required to pay credit fees.
Article 7 Students must exercise their best judgment when taking courses offered in the other
programs. The students are not allowed to add or drop courses once they finish the
course selection process.
Article 8 These Regulations shall be implemented after approval by the Academic Affairs
Conference and subsequent endorsement by the President. The same applies to any
subsequent revisions.

## National Taipei University Principles for Course Offering (Rev. Oct. 2025)
### Page 1
National Taipei University Principles for Course Offering
In case of any discrepancy or inconsistency between the English and Chinese versions of the
regulations, the Chinese version shall take precedence.
Last Revised: Oct. 2025
Article 1. National Taipei University ("the University") formulates the Principles for Course Offering
(the "Principles") to organize course categories according to the following regulations.
I. Each department shall refer to the curriculum structure of three benchmark domestic
or foreign universities with similar departments at least once every four years. This
review shall encompass required professional courses, course credits, and total
graduation credits. The total graduation credits should not exceed the highest total
among the three benchmark universities by more than 3 credits.
II. The graduation credits for undergraduates shall include common core compulsory
courses, required and electives professional courses, and free credit courses as follows:
(I). Common core course credits:
A. For students admitted in the academic years 2010 to 2015, the following
regulations apply, including the Freshman Mandarin Course: Classic
Reading and Interpretation, Foreign Language, History, General Education,
and Physical Education. The Freshman Mandarin Course: Classic Reading
and Interpretation, with 6 credits; Foreign Language, with 6 credits; History,
with 4 credits; General Education, with 12 credits; and Physical Education,
with 0 credits for four semesters.
B. For students admitted in the academic years 2016 to 2017, the following
regulations apply, including General Education and Physical Education.
General Education courses consist of Language General Education and
Dimensional General Education. Language General Education courses
include Freshman Mandarin: Classic Reading and Interpretation, with 4
credits, and Foreign Language, with 6 credits. Dimensional General
Education courses require 18 credits, and Physical Education has 0 credits
over four semesters.
C. For students admitted in the academic years 2018 to 2020, the following
### Page 2
regulations apply, including those for General Education and Physical
Education. General Education courses consist of Language General
Education and Dimensional General Education. Language General
Education courses include Freshman Mandarin: Classic Reading and
Interpretation, with 4 credits, and College English/Adapted College English,
with 4 credits. Dimensional General Education courses require 16 credits,
and Physical Education is assigned 0 credits over four semesters.
D. For students admitted in the academic years 2021 to 2023, the following
regulations apply, including General Education, Physical Education, and
Programming-related courses. General Education courses consist of
Language General Education and Dimensional General Education.
Language General Education courses include Freshman Mandarin: Classic
Reading and Interpretation, with 4 credits, and College English/Adapted
College English, with 4 credits. Dimensional General Education courses
require 16 credits. Physical Education is assigned 0 credits over four
semesters. Programming-related courses require students to either obtain a
University-approved external certification or pass at least one University-
designated course.
E. For students admitted in the academic year 2024, the following regulations
apply, including General Education, Physical Education, and Programming-
related courses. General Education courses consist of Language General
Education and Dimensional General Education. Language General
Education courses include Freshman Mandarin: Classic Reading and
Interpretation I and II, with 4 credits, and College English, with 4 credits.
Dimensional General Education courses require 16 credits. Physical
Education is assigned 0 credits over four semesters. Programming-related
courses require students to either obtain a University-approved external
certification or pass at least one University-designated course.
Undergraduate students in the International College of Sustainability Innovations
### Page 3
and those enrolled in Dual-degree programs at the University shall adhere to
specific regulations regarding the completion of “Common Core Course Credits.”
If their respective departments have additional regulations, those departmental
regulations shall take precedence.
(II). The total required professional course credits in each department shall not exceed
45% of the total graduation credits, which include Common Core course credits,
for the respective department.
(III). The elective professional course credits in each department shall constitute a
quarter of the total graduation credits, and at least 16 credits shall be allocated
for free elective courses.
Article 2. Principles for Course Offering.
I. Except for general education, integration, and minor courses, each department may
offer only one section of the same course per academic year. For departments offering
multiple sections, the number of sections may be doubled. Any request for additional
sections must be submitted to and approved by the school’s curriculum committee and
will be handled separately.
(I). For internships and practical courses that need to be divided into groups due to
venue limitations, equipment constraints, or special circumstances, the instructor
shall have the authority to determine group sizes based on the instructional
requirements of each course.
(II). The foundation courses in the bachelor’s program shall be divided into sections
if the enrollment exceeds 75 students.
(III). If the enrollment for seminar-type courses in the master’s program exceeds 30
students, or the enrollment for the doctoral program exceeds 10 students, the
course will be divided into sections.
(IV).Departments offering a single class in the bachelor’s program may apply to offer
one additional required course taught in English (EMI) per semester.
II. Support for other departments offering the same subjects (such as Statistics, Calculus,
etc.) shall be considered by the supporting department, taking into account the diverse
### Page 4
needs of the supported departments or faculties. This includes categorizing course
content and, where possible, ensuring consistency in the curriculum and teaching
progress.
III. The required professional courses within each department must be taught by full-time
faculty to ensure teaching quality and meet the department’s educational goals and
core competencies. However, for practical courses that require industry experts or
specialized faculty, such cases shall be submitted and approved by the university’s
Curriculum Committee for separate handling.
IV. In order to promote effective student learning, manage teaching workload, and make
full use of classroom resources, courses in each department shall avoid concentration
during the same time periods and shall be evenly distributed across different days.
V. Full-time faculty members are limited to teaching a maximum of two courses per day
within the regular academic program. The regular academic program does not include
the On-the-Job Graduate Program or the Extension Education Bachelor’s Program.
VI. The required and elective courses of each department shall not be scheduled on
Wednesday afternoons during the 5th and 6th periods to allow for the arrangement of
school-wide common courses and various meetings. However, courses that require
large classrooms or computer labs, due to limited campus space, may be scheduled on
Wednesday afternoons during the 5th and 6th periods, with courses requiring large
classrooms scheduled during both periods and those requiring computer labs
scheduled only during the 6th period.
VII. Flexible Courses: To implement innovative teaching methods, instructors may offer
courses in micro-teaching, intensive teaching formats, or student self-directed
learning approaches, depending on the unique nature of the curriculum. Each
department, degree program, institute, office, or center must submit an application
form and course syllabus for review by the relevant three-tier course committee at
least one semester prior to the course's commencement and must obtain approval
before the course can be offered.
The approved courses mentioned above are not constrained by the 18-week duration
### Page 5
of a semester for the teaching period. However, each credit hour taught by an
instructor must still be completed within 18 hours. Each instructor may offer a
maximum of one intensive teaching course per semester.
The implementation, grading, and credit calculation of flexible courses shall be
conducted in accordance with the “Implementation Guidelines for Flexible Courses.”
VIII. After the course selection period begins, departments and instructors shall not be
permitted to change course information arbitrarily unless approved for specific
reasons, in order to protect students’ learning rights.
Article 3. Configuration of Course Capacity and Limitations.
I. The minimum enrollment requirements for course offerings are as follows:
(I). Full-time faculty: a minimum of eight for bachelor’s programs, two for master’s
programs, and one for doctoral programs.
(II). Adjunct faculty: Minimum of ten for bachelor’s programs, two for master’s
programs, and one for doctoral programs.
(III). Bachelor’s Degree Programs Taught in English (EMI): Minimum of six for full-
time faculty members and eight for adjunct faculty members.
(IV).The courses failing to meet the minimum enrollment standards specified above
shall be canceled. Exceptions may be granted upon approval under the following
conditions:
A. Master’s programs with an enrollment quota of 10 or fewer, or specialized
courses such as thesis projects, internships, national defense education, and
teacher education offered by bachelor programs.
B. Master's program courses taken by foreign students.
C. The full-year course started in the first semester; however, there is
insufficient enrollment for the second semester.
D. For an English as a Medium of Instruction (EMI) course, each instructor is
limited to teaching only one course per semester.
E. The Chinese Language Center offers Mandarin courses for foreign students,
### Page 6
with each instructor limited to teaching one course per semester under the
same course title.
F. The Physical Education: Varsity Team course for student-athletes.
G. The “Adapted Physical Education” and “Adapted College English” courses
are designed for students with disabilities.
H. Satellite courses offered in coordination with the Ministry of Education’s
Taiwan Artificial Intelligence College Alliance (TAICA).
I. Required courses offered by each department (degree program or graduate
institute).
II. To encourage interdisciplinary learning and ensure the course selection rights of
double-minor students, each department shall allocate at least 15% of the course
enrollment capacity for double majors, minors, and students from other majors. After
approval by the University-level Curriculum Committee, certain specialized courses
may be restricted to enrollment exclusively for departmental students, including
those pursuing double minors.
During the initial course selection stage, departmental courses with enrollment
exceeding 100 students may reserve an additional 10% of the course capacity for
students pursuing double majors, minors, or those from other majors.
If classroom capacity limitations prevent the allocation of an additional 15% course
selection quota for double major, minor, and other department students, the
Curriculum Section of the Academic Affairs Office shall make separate arrangements
to address the issue.
III. The prerequisites and limitations established by each department are posted on the
course inquiry system to aid students in scheduling their academic progress during
their study period. Departments should avoid frequent changes, which may affect
students’ course selection rights and long-term course selection plans.
IV. To enhance the flexibility and diversity of course selection for the university students,
each department should appropriately review the necessity of course prerequisites and
enrollment limitations to safeguard the students’ learning rights.
Article 4. The regulations mentioned above do not apply to the university’s Extension Education
### Page 7
Bachelor’s Program, On-the-Job Graduate Program, and The Center for Teacher Education.
However, they are subject to the relevant internal regulations of the university.
Article 5. These principles shall be implemented after approval by the Academic Affairs Conference
and subsequent endorsement by the President. The same applies to any subsequent
revisions.

## National Taipei University Regulations Governing the Implementation of InterUniversity Course Selection (Rev. Nov. 2023)
### Page 1
National Taipei University Regulations Governing the Implementation of Inter-
University Course Selection
In case of any discrepancy or inconsistency between the English and Chinese versions of the
regulations, the Chinese version shall take precedence.
Last Revised: November 2023
Article 1. The regulations are formulated in accordance with Article 25 of the Enforcement Rules of
the University Act and Article 24 of the National Taipei University (NTPU or “the
University) Academic Regulations.
Article 2. Students may select courses offered by other universities, limited to those approved by
their respective departments or institutes.
Article 3. The credits for courses taken at another institution each semester may not exceed ⅓ of the
total credits for the current semester. This rule does not apply under the following
circumstances:
(I). Undergraduate students and second-year or higher Extension Education Bachelor
Program students who select courses offered by the University System of Taipei.
(II). Undergraduate students, graduating students, and students with an extended period
of study in the Extension Education Bachelor Program.
(III). Master's and doctoral students.
The calculation of graduation credits, including credits obtained from courses taken at other
institutions shall adhere to the regulations of individual departments.
Article 4. Students taking courses at another institution shall submit an application to the relevant
academic affairs division before the specified course selection deadline. After obtaining
approval from the director of the respective department (institute, center), it should be
forwarded to the academic affairs office for further review. Those who meet the
requirements will be issued an approval letter by the University to complete the course
selection procedure at the respective institution, all the procedures must be completed
before the course add/drop deadline.
Article 5. When students take courses at another university, the payment, instruction, grade
calculation, and other relevant regulations shall adhere to those of the respective
### Page 2
institution. The class schedule (including transportation time) should not overlap with the
course schedule at the University; violators will have their scores for the course marked
as zero.
The payment for students who have taken a course offered by the University System of
Taipei will be handled in accordance with a signed reciprocal agreement. Students who
are required to pay credit fees at the offering institution will not be charged by the
University. However, for those students exempted from credit fees at the offering
institution, the credits will be combined with the University, and fees will be charged
according to the university's fee regulations.
Article 6. Students from other institutions applying to take courses offered by the University are
required to submit an approval letter issued by their respective institution. Applications
must be submitted to the relevant academic affairs office of the University before the
add/drop deadline and must obtain approval from the department offering the course.
Late applications will not be accepted.
Course selection is limited to the maximum number of student quota set by each
department (institutes) of the University, and students are required to pay the credit fees
according to the University’s regulations. If the course includes internships, additional
internship fees should be paid.
Article 7. After the end of each semester, the academic affairs office shall forward the grades to
students' respective institutions for grade recordation.
Article 8. These Regulations shall be implemented after approval by the Academic Affairs
Conference and subsequent endorsement by the President. The same applies to any
subsequent revisions.

## National Taipei University Regulations for Course Selection. (Rev. Oct. 2025)
### Page 1
National Taipei University Regulations for Course Selection
In case of any discrepancy or inconsistency between the English and Chinese versions of the
regulations, the Chinese version shall take precedence.
Last Revised: October 2025
Article 1. The Regulations for Course Selection (“the Regulations”) were established in accordance
with Article 21 of the National Taipei University Academic Regulations.
Article 2. The course selection process, including the selection dates, methods, and course categories,
shall be carried out in accordance with the specific dates indicated on the University's
academic calendar. The Academic Office may establish additional guidelines for student
course selection as deemed necessary.
Instructors may restrict the number of elective students based on classroom capacity,
equipment availability, the nature of the course, and other professional relevant
considerations.
Article 3. The required number of credits per semester for undergraduate students in each department
shall be as follows: First, second, and third-year students shall not take fewer than 16 credits
per semester. Fourth-year students (excluding extension students) shall not take fewer than
9 credits per semester.
Undergraduate program students who exceed 25 credits, minor students who exceed 28
credits, double major students who exceed 31 credits, or students retaking a course for the
second time are required to pay an additional credit fee.
For Extension Education Bachelor students in a four-year program, the minimum number
of credits to be taken per semester shall not be fewer than 16. Graduating students
(excluding those who extend their years of study) shall not take fewer than 9 credits. For
students in a five-year program, the minimum number of credits to be taken per semester
shall not be fewer than 9, and graduating students (excluding those who extend their years
of study) shall not take fewer than 5 credits.
On-the-Job Graduate Program students shall take at least one course each semester, and the
selected courses must be recognized as graduation credits by the department (institute),
excluding students in their second year or above.
Students may reduce their course credits by a maximum of 4 credits in the current semester
### Page 2
with the approval of the department director, due to the following special circumstances.
Students who obtain approval from the Dean of the Academic Affairs Office or the Director
of the Extension Education Center may be exempt from the minimum credit requirement.
However, they must still enroll in at least one course.
I. Students who are approved for a suspension of studies during the first semester.
II. Students who failed to meet the academic requirements in the previous semester, with
scores below 40 in multiple subjects, will be unable to continue their courses in the
upcoming semester.
III. Due to physical health reasons.
IV. Students who are unable to take other courses due to course exemptions.
V. Graduating students (excluding those who extend their years of study) who can fulfill
the graduation requirements by taking fewer than nine credits in both the first and
second semesters.
VI. Other Special Circumstances.
Students approved by the University's 'Regulation for Courses Taken Abroad by Students'
to study abroad shall adhere to the credit requirements per semester outlined in the same
regulation, without being subject to the limitations mentioned in the previous article.
Article 4. For full-year courses, students who have not completed the first semester or who have
obtained a score below 40 are ineligible to enroll in the second semester of the course.
Failure to comply will result in no credits or grades being recorded. However, exemptions
may be granted by each department (institute, office, center) if the content of the full-year
course is discontinuous or due to curriculum adjustments, provided that students do not
continuously retake the course.
For full-year courses, if students only complete the first semester without proceeding to the
second semester, or if they fail the course in the second semester, the credits earned in the
first semester will not be counted towards graduation credits.
In the event that students repeatedly register for a course with the same curriculum name
as one that has been enrolled, passed, or exempted, the credits will not be counted towards
the minimum graduation credits. However, individual departments (institutes, offices,
### Page 3
centers) may grant exceptions in accordance with other regulations, which are not bound
by this limitation.
The sequence and recognition of courses from different subjects are determined by the
respective director of the department (institute, office, center).
Course selection for Overseas non-degree program students is exempt from the restrictions
on grade, status, or prerequisite subjects and is not bound by the minimum credit
requirements of the preceding article. However, any additional regulations imposed by the
offering department or instructor shall take precedence.
Course selection for Domestic exchange students must adhere to the regulations outlined
for course selection of domestic exchange students.
Article 5. Students should prudently choose their courses in advance and verify with the department
(institute ) if the credits are recognized as graduation credits. Students must complete the
add/drop of courses online before the deadline indicated on the academic calendar of the
University for course add/drop.
Students should save or print a copy of their course selection results after registering for
courses at each stage of course selection, and check the course selection results published
in the online course selection system on the designated date to confirm the registered course
and credits. For students who do not complete online verification, the course shall be based
on the course selection system.
In the following circumstances, students may fill out the application form for course
add/drop due to significant reasons, have it signed and approved by the course instructor
and department office, and submit it to the competent division of the Office of Academic
Affairs by the stipulated deadline.
I. Bachelor program and Extension Education Bachelor program students:
(I). In the event that the grade has not yet been submitted by the instructor, or if the
students are blocked from registering or have a score below 40, they may be
unable to register for the course.
(II). Late approvals for course exemptions.
(III). Courses with conflicting schedules.
### Page 4
(IV).The course that was originally selected was canceled.
(V). Graduating students will not be able to graduate in the current academic year
without taking this course; they may only add a course if the schedule is not full.
(VI).Students who have not met the minimum credit requirement for elective courses
may only add courses that are not full.
(VII). Overseas non-degree program students and domestic exchange students may
only add courses that are not full in the same schedule.
(VIII). Extension education bachelor program students who ranked in the top 20%
of their department in the previous semester may only add courses from the
bachelor program.
(IX).Transfer students and students changing their major who need to take
professional required courses from previous grades may only add courses that
are not full in the same schedule.
(X). Transfer students and students changing their major, who have been exempted
from their department's full-year required courses and need to make up for
courses in the following semester, may only add courses that are not full in the
same schedule.
(XI).Foreign students who must add courses that are taught in English.
II. Master's and Doctoral Program students, and On-the-Job Graduate Program students:
(I). In the event that the grade has not yet been submitted by the instructor, or the
students are blocked from registering or have a score below 40, they may not be
able to register for the course.
(II). Late approvals for course exemptions.
(III). Courses with conflicting schedules.
(IV).The course that was originally selected was canceled.
(V). Graduating students will not be able to graduate in the current academic year
without taking this course; they may only add a course if the schedule is not full.
(VI).Overseas non-degree program students and domestic exchange students may
only add courses that are not full in the same schedule.
### Page 5
(VII). Master's students who are required to take foundational credits from their
department.
(VIII). Foreign students who must add courses that are taught in English.
III. Except for the two subparagraphs, the student is required to provide relevant evidence
and reasons for any other special circumstances that are not attributable to them. The
request must be signed and approved by the course instructor, the course offering unit,
the department director, the Dean of the Academic Affairs Office, or the Director of
the Extension Education Center, and submitted to the competent division of the
Academic Affairs Office.
After one-third of the teaching hours have passed, except for course selection errors or
other circumstances not attributable to students, the selected courses cannot be modified
for any reason. However, students may withdraw from one course after the midterm
examination in accordance with the provisions of Article 14 of the regulations.
Article 6. For courses offered by different departments or conducted in separate classes or groups,
students must select courses according to the prescribed academic year, department
(institute), class, or group. Without approval from the department (or institute), students
are not allowed to select or request changes to departments (or institutes), classes, or groups
during course selection. However, for courses designated with alternative regulations,
students may select courses according to those regulations.
Article 7. Required courses shall not be changed, dropped, or deferred, except when the course
schedule conflicts with the course that the student is required to retake, or with the approval
of the department (or institute).
Article 8. To meet academic requirements, master's students may select relevant courses offered to
third- and fourth-year students in other departments. With approval from both the
department directors and the directors of the departments offering the course, these credits
may be recognized toward graduation credits, with a maximum of 3 credits. Doctoral
students who select master's program courses may have these credits counted toward their
total credits.
The credits and grades earned by master's and doctoral degree students in elective bachelor
### Page 6
program and extension education bachelor program courses will not be included in the
semester grade point average or counted toward graduation credits. However, if they do
not conflict with the provisions outlined in the first paragraph of this article, students may
receive a certificate of completion for the undergraduate courses.
Article 9. Students in their third year (or above) of bachelor degree programs or extension education
bachelor programs may enroll in master's program courses if they have earned a grade point
average of 80 (or above) in the previous semester or rank in the top 10% of their class, and
have obtained approval from the course instructor, class advisor, department director, and
the director of the department (or institute) offering the course. Enrollment is limited to a
maximum of 6 credits per semester (with no limit in the graduation year). However,
master's program courses must be selected by master's or doctoral students in order to be
offered.
The preceding paragraph is not applicable to pre-master's students who are in a bachelor-
to-master's degree program. The criteria and procedures for applying to the master's
program shall be in accordance with the University's Regulations for Bachelor-to-Master's
Degree Programs and the regulations established by each department (institute).
Article 10. Bachelor program student may select extension education bachelor program’s courses
and vice versa due to special requirements, and the regulations shall be separately
established.
Article 11. In the event that the University changes the name of course, or increases/decreases the
number of credits, or converts full-year courses to half-year courses, half-year courses
to full-year courses, or three-semester courses to full-year courses, the new course will
apply to students who need to retake courses. The recognition of credits earned during
the transition period between the old and new subject catalog shall be based on the
relevant regulations of the respective departments (institutes, offices, centers).
The calculation of times a student retakes a course is based on the course name. If the
course name is the same, each retake is counted, regardless of the number of credits.
However, if credits are considered, the number of retakes are not counted.
Article 12. Students may select courses offered by other universities or overseas universities as
### Page 7
needed. Regulations for inter-university course selection and taking courses overseas
shall be separately established.
The course selection and credit recognition regulations for students enrolled in courses
offered by the Ministry of Education’s “Taiwan AI College Alliance, TAICA” shall be
handled in accordance with the “National Taipei University AI Alliance Credit Program
Course Selection Guidelines. ”
Article 13. Deleted.
Article 14. Students who are unable to continue their studies may apply for dropping from the course
after course selection confirmation.
Students shall fill out an application form for dropping from courses and submit the
application online. The dropping procedures will only be completed after obtaining
approval from the student's department (institute) director and notifying the course
instructor.
The course drop process shall be completed within two weeks after midterm
examination in accordance with the academic calendar of the University.
Students are allowed to drop a maximum of 2 courses per semester, and the total number
of credits shall not fall below the minimum credit requirement stated in Article 3 of this
regulation. For students who have extended their study period, they shall take at least
one course after dropping process.
In addition to the preceding paragraph, the number of credits after course dropping
process must still comply with other course selection related regulations of the
University.
Article 15. The procedures for grading, credit calculation, and credit payment for courses dropped
by students shall be handled in accordance with the following regulations:
I. The dropped courses still need to be recorded on the transcript for the semester and
transcript of academic year record with a “W” (Withdraw) in the grade column.
II. The dropped courses still need to be recorded on the transcript for the semester and
academic year transcript, with a "W" (Withdraw) in the grade column.
III. In accordance with the regulations, for courses dropped after the payment of tuition
### Page 8
and fees (including credit and miscellaneous fees), the fees shall not be refunded, and
any unpaid fees shall still be required to be paid.
Article 16. The minimum enrollment number for each course to be offered at the University shall
be handled in accordance with the following regulations:
I. Full-time faculty members: eight or more for bachelor programs and extension
education bachelor programs; two or more for master’s programs and on-the-job
graduate programs; one or more for doctoral programs.
II. Adjunct faculty members: ten or more for bachelor programs and extension
education bachelor programs; two or more for master’s programs and on-the-job
graduate programs; one or more for doctoral programs.
III. English as a Medium of Instruction (EMI) for bachelor programs and extension
education bachelor programs: six or more for full-time faculty members; eight or
more for adjunct faculty members.
Courses that do not meet the minimum enrollment requirement stated above shall not
be offered. Exceptions may be granted with approval under the following conditions:
I. Master’s programs with an enrollment quota of 10 or fewer, or specialized courses
such as thesis projects, internships, national defense education, and teacher
education offered by bachelor programs and extension education bachelor
programs.
II. Master's program courses taken by foreign students.
III. The full-year course started in the first semester; however, there is insufficient
enrollment for the second semester.
IV. For an English as a Medium of Instruction (EMI) course, each instructor is limited
to teaching only one course per semester.
V. The Chinese Language Center offers Mandarin courses for foreign students, with
each instructor limited to teaching one course per semester under the same course
title.
VI. The Physical Education: Varsity Team course for student-athletes.
VII. The “Adapted Physical Education” and “Adapted College English” courses are
### Page 9
designed for students with disabilities.
VIII. Satellite courses offered in coordination with the Ministry of Education’s “ Taiwan
AI College Alliance, TAICA.”
IX. Required courses offered by each department (degree program or graduate institute).
Article 17. These Regulations shall be implemented after approval by the Academic Affairs
Conference and subsequent endorsement by the President. The same applies to any
subsequent revisions.

## National Taipei University Regulations for the Calculation of Faculty Member's Teaching Hours and Overtime Payment (Rev. April 2025)
### Page 1
National Taipei University Regulations for the Calculation of Faculty
Members’ Teaching Hours and Overtime Payment
In case of any discrepancy or inconsistency between the English and Chinese versions of
the regulations, the Chinese version shall take precedence.
Last Revised: April 2025
Article 1. The National Taipei University (NTPU or “the University”) hereby formulates the
NTPU Regulations for Calculating Teaching Hours and Overtime Payment for
Faculty (“the Regulations”) in accordance with Article 18 of the Enforcement Rules
of the University Act to judiciously conduct matters related to the calculation of full-
time faculty members’ weekly teaching hours and overtime pay.
Article 2. The basic weekly teaching hours required of full-time faculty members at the
University (“faculty member(s)”) per semester are specified as follows:
I. 8 hours for Professors.
II. 9 hours for Associate professors.
III. 9 hours for Assistant professors.
IV. 10 hours for Lecturers.
According to Article 10 of the National Taipei University Regulations for Faculty
Appointment and Promotion, the basic teaching hours per week for newly hired
lecturers, assistant professors, and associate professors during the first three years
of each semester may be reduced by three hours. Excess hours from non-foreign
language courses shall not be eligible for additional overtime pay. Only under the
conditions stipulated in Article 7, a maximum of 4 hours can be calculated for
overtime pay, and other forms of reductions are also not permissible.
Newly hired faculty who do not receive a promotion within the specified period,
and upon one contract extension still cannot be promoted, are required to reimburse
the teaching hours that were previously reduced during the first three years of their
initial appointment within three years, and they shall not be eligible for additional
overtime pay until the effective date of the promotion. Newly hired faculty members
### Page 2
who experienced a reduction in teaching hours and were simultaneously involved
in National Science and Technology Council projects or supervising graduate
students may submit relevant proof to offset the hours that need to be reimbursed
for the initial three years. However, the application of credit hours for newly hired
faculty is not applicable, starting from the academic year 2017.
The basic weekly teaching hours include full-time faculty members teaching in the
bachelor's program, master's program (excluding the On-the-Job Graduate Program),
and doctoral program. Calculations for faculty members who exceed or fall short of
their teaching hours may be conducted annually by averaging the teaching hours for
both semesters in each academic year.
Faculty members who are short of weekly teaching hours shall make up the
difference in the following academic year or until the teaching hour requirement is
met, and no additional overtime pay will be provided.
Article 3. Faculty members who also serve as academic affairs or administrative official at the
University may be granted a reduction in their basic teaching hours in accordance
with the following standards:
I. The faculty members may be granted a reduction of 4 teaching hours:
(I). Faculty members serving as a Vice President, a Dean of Academic
Affairs, an Associate Dean of Academic Affairs, a Dean of Student
Affairs, an Associate Dean of Student Affairs, a Dean of General
Affairs, an Associate Dean of General Affairs, a Dean of Research
and Development, Associate Dean of Research and Development,
a Dean of International Affairs, Associate Dean of International
Affairs, Deans of respective colleges, a Chief Secretary, a Library
Director, a Dean of Extension Education Center, a Director of
General Education Center, a Director of Sports Affairs, a Director
of Information Center, a Director of Alumni Center.
### Page 3
(II). Faculty members serving as Section Chiefs and Directors in the Office
of Academic Affairs, the Office of Student Affairs, the Office of General
Affairs, the Office of Research and Development, the Office of
International Affairs, and the Office of the Secretariat.
(III). Faculty members serving as Directors of the Alumni Center, the
Information Technology Center, the Extension Education Center, and the
Physical Education Division.
(IV). Faculty members serving as secretaries in the Office of the Vice President
and in other colleges.
(V). Departments without designated teaching assistant positions may have
one staff member handling secretarial duties, limited to one per
department.
(VI). Faculty members serving as Directors in the functional office with the
approval of the Administrative Council and validated by the President.
II. Faculty members may be granted a reduction of 3 teaching hours: faculty
members serving as Directors of each department, the degree programs, the
graduate institute, the Center for Teacher Education in the College of
Humanities, the Chinese Language Centers in the International College of
Sustainability Innovation, and non-department heads concurrently serving as
associate deans of a single department within a college.
III. Faculty members may be granted a reduction of 2 teaching hours: faculty
members serving as Directors of the Center for Institutional Research, and
research centers in each college.
IV. Faculty members may be granted a reduction of 1 teaching hour: faculty
members serving as Coordinators of each credit program. However,
coordinators of interdisciplinary micro-programs have a reduction of 0.5 hours.
Article 4. Faculty members who fail to meet the basic teaching hours may be acknowledged
### Page 4
as follows: the hours will not be counted as additional overtime hours.
I. Faculty members who received the Outstanding Research Incentive Award
from the National Science and Technology Council may be granted a reduction
of 3 teaching hours in the following academic year.
II. Faculty members conducting research projects funded by the National Science
and Technology Council or non-National Science Council projects, including
Industry Cooperation, with an administrative management fee allocation of
NTD 150,000 or more per project during the project execution period (counted
from the project start year, with a reservation period of three years), may be
granted a reduction of 1 teaching hour for each project. This reduction applies
to either the principal investigator or co-principal investigator receiving the
project management fee. The maximum reduction is two projects, and the
reduction of teaching hours is limited to two hours per semester.
III. Faculty members conducting projects funded by the Ministry of Education and
commissioned projects with a project budget of NTD 1,000,000 or more may
be granted a reduction of 1 teaching hour per semester (counted from the
project start year, with a reservation period of three years).
IV. Faculty members serving as thesis advisors at the University may be granted a
reduction of 1 teaching hour per student. The reduction is limited to one
academic year for master’s students and two academic years for doctoral
students, with a maximum reduction of 2 hours each semester.
The total hours from the aforementioned reductions are limited to 3 hours per
semester.
V. Faculty members who teach in the Extension Education Bachelor Program at
the University may supplement their basic teaching hours with the teaching
hours for the Extension Education Bachelor Program courses. The limit is one
course per semester with a maximum of three hours. However, the
### Page 5
supplemented hours will not qualify for additional teaching payment.
The basic teaching hours supplemented by the Extension Education Bachelor
Program, as stated in item 5, shall not exceed 27 credits per department per week
per semester (36 credits for the Department of Business Administration and the
Department of Public Administration and Policy).
VI. Faculty members who offer courses in the Summer Session for the Bachelor’s
program or Pre-University Bachelor’s program matching the Article 5, Section
1 of the University Regulations for Offering Summer Courses and upon
obtaining approval from the Department Director, may have these hours
counted as basic teaching hours for the next academic year. However, the
teaching hours will not qualify for additional overtime payment in the current
academic year and are also limited to 2 courses and 6 credits per academic year.
Article 5. Faculty members excluding the President are required to conduct a minimum of 3
physical teaching hours per week in each semester for the day program (excluding
weighted teaching hours). In accordance with the provisions of Article 3 and 4,
Section 1 to 4, the total accumulated reduction weekly teaching hours are maximum
of 4 hours per semester.
Article 6. Courses that fail to meet the minimum enrollment standards due to specific
conditions stated in Article 16 of the Regulations for Student Course Selection and
have obtained approval for renewal may use the original calculation of teaching
hours for the required courses (single class) in each department, the satellite
courses offered in collaboration with the Ministry of Education’s “Taiwan
Artificial Intelligence College Alliance (TAICA),” and Mandarin courses
offered by the Chinese Language Center for foreign students. For other courses, the
determination of teaching hourly payment is as follows:
I. The course taught by faculty members: it will be included in the basic teaching
hours, and if there is any overtime payment, it may be allocated based on the
proportion of the course's enrollment numbers to the minimum enrollment
### Page 6
standard.
II. The course taught by adjunct faculty: hourly payment may be allocated based
on the proportion of the course's enrollment numbers to the minimum
enrollment standard.
The term "Minimum enrollment standard" mentioned in the preceding clause is
calculated according to Article 16 of the Regulations for Student Course Selection.
The funding source for the first item of hourly fees is as follows:
I. Funded by the General Expenses of each offering department. However, for
courses offered to other departments or institutes, the General Expenses of the
supported unit shall be responsible.
II. The following courses are funded by institutional funds.
(I). Departments (or graduate programs) with an admission quota of fewer
than 10 students for the master's program.
(II). Professional internship courses in the workplace.
(III). Degree Program in English, Credit Program taught in English.
(IV). Mandarin courses offered by the Chinese Language Center for
International students.
(V). “Adapted Physical Education” and “Adapt to College Life”.
(VI). The Center for Teacher Education offered the courses in “Teaching
Methods and Materials”, “Practice Teaching” and “Educational Practice”.
However, each faculty member is limited to one course per academic
year.
(VII). National Defense Education.
(VIII). Courses continued in the second semester of the full academic year,
for which the enrollment in the first semester has met the minimum
enrollment requirement.
Article 7. Teaching hours shall be calculated as follows and may be included in the overtime
### Page 7
hours calculation:
I. The teaching hours for instructors are calculated based on class enrollment, an
additional 1.2 times for course with 75-89 registered students, 1.5 times for a
course with 90 students, and 2 times for a course with more than 120 students.
II. Deleted.
III. The “Massive Open Online Courses (MOOCs)” approved and offered in the
Bachelor’s and Extension Education Bachelor Program, when integrated with
physical classes (SPOCs), teaching hours will be calculated for instructors as
18 weeks for the initial implementation; for subsequent implementations, the
teaching hours will be calculated as 6 weeks.
IV. The courses approved for teaching in a foreign language shall be calculated at
a 1.5 times weighted rate in accordance with Article 4 of the Directives for the
Implementation of Subsidies for Teaching in a Foreign Language.
V. The teaching hours for instructors co-taught in an approved interdisciplinary
course shall be assigned to individual instructors based on their respective
actual weekly hours taught.
The maximum teaching hours for 2 co-taught instructors are twice the number
of credits assigned to the course.
The teaching hours for 3 or more co-taught instructors, and the student-to-
teacher ratio reaches 8:1, the maximum hours for the course is three times the
standard rate. The student-to-teacher ratio is below 8:1, the maximum hours
for the course is twice the standard rate.
VI. The University Social Responsibility Project courses which have been
reviewed and approved, shall be calculated at 1.5 times the standard rate. The
overtime payment shall be covered by the expenditure from the Ministry of
Education’s Higher Education Sprout Project funds.
For the same subject, if calculating the teaching hours based on the
### Page 8
regulations specified in the preceding six clauses, only one weighted
calculation method is permissible.
VII. The general course "Freshman Chinese: Classical Reading and Interpretation"
for Overseas Chinese Student, an additional 1 hour for composition per class
each semester, totaling three hours. However, the general course "Freshman
Chinese: Classical Reading and Interpretation" for non-overseas Chinese
students and the Extension Education Bachelor Program shall not include the
composition hour.
VIII. The teaching hours for internships and practical sessions are counted based on
the course attributes outlined in the curriculum planning table of each
department (or institute).
IX. The satellite courses offered in collaboration with the Ministry of Education’s
“Taiwan Artificial Intelligence College Alliance (TAICA)” may be
compensated based on the actual number of teaching hours. However, each
faculty member is limited to one such course per semester.
Article 8. The overtime reimbursement method for faculty members is allocated based on the
actual teaching hours, calculated by averaging the teaching hours for both semesters,
and disbursed collectively in the next semester.
Faculty members who meet the total hours eligible for overtime payment due to
exceeding the standard teaching hours can receive overtime payment with maximum
four hours per week. However, in cases where instructors exceed the limit in the
current semester due to large class instruction or teaching in a foreign language, the
maximum overtime hour can be extended to six hours.
Article 9. The teaching hours for adjunct faculty are limited to four hours per week.
Retired faculty members transitioning to adjunct roles for Extension Education
Bachelor Program courses are not subject to the 4-hour teaching limit. Instead, they
are allowed to teach for a maximum of 8 hours per week, similar to other faculty
members.
Adjunct faculty who are not civil servants or faculty members at other universities
may be allotted an additional 4 hours if they teach General Education courses,
### Page 9
Language Center courses, or Chinese Language Center courses. They may also be
allotted an additional 2 hours if they teach required courses for Minors, Double
Majors, or foundational courses for programs. However, the total teaching hours for
day and night classes should not exceed 12 hours per week.
Article 10. The hourly fees for adjunct faculty who teach in Bachelor’s, Master’s, and
Doctoral programs are provided based on the teaching hours, and in accordance
with the “Hourly Payment Standards for Adjunct Instructors in Public Colleges
and Universities”. However, for teaching in the Extension Education Bachelor
Program, the hourly fees are provided based on the teaching hours as per the
University’s “Hourly Payment Standards for Adjunct Instructors in the Extension
Education Center”.
The hourly fees are based on the teaching hours and disbursed monthly in
accordance with the provisions of Article 7, and overtime beyond the allotted
teaching hours are not included. However, adjunct faculty who are not civil
servants or faculty members at other universities may receive additional overtime
payment calculated according to the provisions of Article 7.
Article 11. The contract teaching instructors hired under the “National Taipei University
Regulations for the Appointment of Contract Teaching Instructors” and project
instructors employed by the Ministry of Education’s Higher Education Sprout
Project may be eligible for a maximum of 4 hours of overtime pay per week if
their teaching hours exceed the allocated hours, taking into account the weighted
calculation hours for foreign language and larger classes.
Article 12. Faculty members are limited to a total of 16 teaching hours per week, including
those courses in all degree program on and off-campus, and courses taught at the
Extension Education Center, excluding teaching hours for the Extension
Education Bachelor’s program.
Article 13. These regulations shall be proposed for approval at the Academic Affairs
Conferences, presented to the University Council for approval, and subsequently
### Page 10
submitted to the President for implementation. The same procedure applies to any
subsequent revisions.

## National Taipei University Regulations for Taking Credit Programs (Rev. Oct. 2025)
### Page 1
National Taipei University Regulations for Taking Credit Programs
In case of any discrepancy or inconsistency between the English and Chinese versions of the
regulations, the Chinese version shall take precedence.
Last Revised: October 2025
Article 1 These Regulations are established in accordance with Article 28 of the University Act and
Article 22 of the National Taipei University Academic Regulations.
Article 2 To cultivate cross-disciplinary talents, the various teaching and research units of National
Taipei University (hereinafter referred to as "the University") may establish inter-college,
inter-departmental, or inter-graduate institute credit programs or degree programs to
provide students with opportunities to take cross-disciplinary courses. These Regulations
are hereby established.
Article 3 Matters regarding eligibility for taking programs and curriculum requirements shall be
handled in accordance with the National Taipei University Regulations for Establishing
Programs and the specific regulations established for each program.
Article 4 The regulations governing the number of credits for students taking credit programs are as
follows; however, individual programs may establish stricter requirements: I. Cross-
disciplinary Credit Programs: Must be no fewer than 15 credits. II. Micro-programs: Must
be no fewer than 8 credits.
Among these, at least 6 credits must be for subjects that are not required courses of the
student's major department, double major department, or minor department. Furthermore,
the subjects applied for accreditation must meet the following conditions: I. Cross-
disciplinary Credit Programs: At least two professional subjects not from the student's
major department. II. Micro-programs: At least one professional subject not from the
student's major department.
The criteria for determining "professional subjects not from the student's major
department" shall be based on the curriculum map (professional subject list) of the
student's major department. If a subject listed in the table is taken across departments, it
shall still be regarded as a professional subject of the major department.
Graduation credit requirements and other mandatory matters for degree programs shall
comply with the regulations governing degrees at each level.
Article 5 The credits and course grades of the program must be included in the calculation of the
total credits taken and the semester grades for that semester. The upper and lower limits of
credits taken by students per semester shall still be handled in accordance with relevant
course selection regulations.
### Page 2
Article 6 Students may not apply for an extension of their study period on the grounds of not having
completed the required subject credits for a credit program.
For degree programs that recruit students externally, the duration of study shall be handled
in accordance with the relevant provisions of the University's Academic Regulations.
For degree programs provided internally for current students to pursue as a double major,
the duration of study shall be handled in accordance with the relevant provisions of the
National Taipei University Regulations for Students Pursuing Double Majors.
Article 7 Students of the University or other universities who have completed the subject credits
prescribed by a credit program may apply to the credit program establishment unit for the
issuance of a Credit Program Certificate of Completion. After verification by the credit
program establishment unit and upon approval by the Dean of Academic Affairs and the
President, the Certificate of Completion shall be issued by the university-level
administrative unit.
The issuance of Certificates of Completion for cross-university credit programs shall be
handled in accordance with the provisions of the preceding paragraph.
With the consent of the credit program establishment unit, students in the Master’s
programs, Doctoral programs, or In-service Master’s programs of the University may make
up or complete the subject credits of a credit program and apply for the issuance of a Credit
Program Certificate of Completion in accordance with the provisions of the first paragraph.
For those who have completed the subject credits prescribed by a degree program, the
review of their graduation qualifications shall be handled mutatis mutandis in accordance
with the relevant regulations for Bachelor's programs, Continuing Education Bachelor's
programs, and Master's/Doctoral programs.
Article 8 The formats for the Credit Program Certificate of Completion and the Degree Program
Certificate shall be prescribed by the Office of Academic Affairs.
Article 9 These Regulations do not apply to the Teacher Education Program, which shall be handled
in accordance with the National Taipei University Regulations for Taking Teacher
Education Programs and other relevant regulations.
Article 10 Matters not provided for in these Regulations shall be handled in accordance with the
University's Academic Regulations and relevant laws and regulations of the Ministry of
Education.
Article 11 These Regulations shall be implemented after being approved by the Academic Affairs
Meeting and ratified by the President; the same applies to amendments.

## National Taipei University Regulations for Students Taking Courses Overseas (112.10 revised)
### Page 1
National Taipei University Regulations for Students Taking
Courses Overseas
In case of any discrepancy or inconsistency between the English and Chinese
versions of the regulations, the Chinese version shall take precedence.
Revised on October 11,2023
Article 1. These Regulations are established pursuant to Article 24 of the National Taipei University
Academic Regulations.
Article 2. Students applying to take courses at overseas universities shall meet one of the following
conditions:
I. The overseas university has an academic cooperation agreement with National Taipei
University.
II. The overseas university is listed in the Ministry of Education’s reference list of accredited
foreign institutions, or is recognized by the relevant local competent authority or
professional organization, and its credentials have been verified by an ROC overseas
representative office.
Article 3. These Regulations apply to the following students:
I. Undergraduate programs and bachelor’s programs for continuing education: limited to
second-year students and above; students on leave are not eligible.
II. (I). Master’s programs, in-service master’s programs, and doctoral programs: Master’s
and in-service master’s programs: first-, second-, and third-year students.
(II). Doctoral programs: first- through fourth-year students.
Article 4. Students applying to take courses at overseas institutions shall submit the required application
documents to their home department (graduate institute). After preliminary review and approval
by the department chair, the application shall be forwarded to the college dean for approval,
and then reviewed by the Office of International Affairs, the Office of Academic Affairs (or the
Division of Continuing and Extension Education), and the Office of Student Affairs. Upon
approval by the President, the student shall be notified to proceed with overseas study
procedures. The number of students permitted to apply for overseas study shall be determined
by each department (graduate institute) based on actual conditions.
Article 5. Students may take courses overseas for up to two terms. For undergraduate programs, bachelor’s
programs for continuing education, master’s programs, in-service master’s programs, and
doctoral programs, the total period shall not exceed two years. During the overseas study period,
students must complete registration at NTPU in accordance with relevant regulations:
I. I. Undergraduate students with extended study and students in bachelor’s programs for
### Page 2
continuing education shall pay at least the minimum credit fees pursuant to Article 6.
II. Master’s, doctoral, and in-service master’s students shall pay at least the minimum
credit fees and the base tuition and fees pursuant to Article 6.
III. After returning, students in bachelor’s programs for continuing education, master’s
programs, in-service master’s programs, and doctoral programs shall pay any difference
in credit fees if the number of credits recognized exceeds the minimum number required.
Students required to pay tuition and fees to partner institutions under inter-university
cooperation agreements shall be exempt from paying NTPU tuition during the overseas
study period, but must still pay accident insurance and miscellaneous fees (or base
tuition and fees).
Article 6. During overseas study, students must pass at least 6 credits and 2 courses per semester, and
shall not exceed the maximum course load permitted by the host institution. Students must
submit course information and grades to their home department (graduate institute) for
verification, after which the information shall be forwarded to the academic affairs unit for
record. Students who fail to complete the required number of credits shall return any
scholarship funds received.
Article 7. Credit transfer for courses taken at overseas institutions shall be handled as follows:
I. Students shall obtain an official transcript listing all courses and credits earned ,and
submit an application for credit recognition to their home department (graduate institute)
within two months of returning. Approved courses shall be forwarded to the Curriculum
Section (or Continuing Education Section) for course data entry and then to the
Registration Section for credit transfer. Students who fail to submit transcripts within
two months after returning, or who fail to meet the minimum course load requirement
overseas, shall have the remark: “Courses taken overseas during this period were not
submitted for credit recognition.” recorded in their academic transcript.
II. Credit calculation:
(I). One NTPU credit equals 18 hours of instruction; documentation of course hours is
required.
(II). For institutions using the European Credit Transfer System (ECTS), 2 ECTS shall
be recognized as 1 NTPU credit.
(III). Departments (graduate institutes/programs) may adjust credit recognition based on
course syllabi, class hours, or other approved regulations.
III. Limits on transfer credits:
Recognition of course titles and credits, and whether they may be counted toward
graduation requirements, shall be determined by each department (graduate institute).
Transferable credits may not exceed one-fourth of the minimum number required for
### Page 3
graduation; for dual-degree programs, the limit is one-half. Credit transfer for courses
taken in Mainland China, Hong Kong, or Macao shall be handled in accordance with
Ministry of Education regulations.
Article 8. Male students subject to military service shall comply with the “Regulations for Exit of Draftees.”
Required documents must be prepared no later than two months before departure and submitted
to the department (graduate institute) for preliminary review. After approval by the department
chair and college dean, and review by relevant units of the Office of Academic Affairs and
Office of Student Affairs, the application shall be submitted to the President for approval. The
Office of Student Affairs shall then submit the case to the county/city government of the
student’s household registration for exit review. Approval is required before the student may
depart for overseas study.
Matters related to academic affairs and student status not specified in these Regulations shall
be handled in accordance with other applicable regulations.
Article 9.
.
Article 10 These Regulations shall be implemented upon approval by the Academic Affairs Meeting and
ratification by the President. Amendments shall follow the same procedure.

## National Taipei University AI Alliance Credit Program Course Selection Guidelines (Rev. Oct. 2025)
### Page 1
National Taipei University AI Alliance Credit Program Course Selection
Guidelines.
In case of any discrepancy or inconsistency between the English and Chinese versions of the
regulations, the Chinese version shall take precedence.
Revised on October 8, 2025.
Article 1. The National Taipei University (hereinafter referred to as the University or NTPU) has
established these Course Selection Guidelines (hereinafter referred to as the Guidelines) to
align with the Ministry of Education’s regulations on course selection and offerings for the
“Taiwan Artificial Intelligence College Alliance (TAICA).” These Guidelines are designed
to assist students in selecting appropriate courses. Students who complete the required
credits will be eligible to apply for certification of the credit program offered by the AI
Alliance Credit Program.
Article 2. The categories of courses offered by the TAICA are outlined as follows:
I. Master Courses: These are exemplary courses selected by the TAICA office through
expert reviews and offered by the alliance universities (either NTPU or other
institutions) as designated by the TAICA office.
II. Satellite Courses: These courses are offered at the University and utilize resources
from the Master Courses.
III. Regular Courses: These courses are offered by the University and are similar to the
Master Courses. They can be recognized through the University's relevant
procedures and will be reviewed and approved by the TAICA office.
Article 3. Students enrolling in the “Master Courses” offered by other universities under the AI
Alliance Credit Program shall adhere to the following course selection guidelines:
I. The following course selection restrictions do not apply at the University:
(I). The number of credits selected from inter-university courses shall not exceed
one-third of the total credits taken during the semester.
(II). The number of course credits jointly selected by students in the Undergraduate
and Extension Education Bachelor programs shall not exceed one-third of the
### Page 2
total credits taken in a semester.
(III). The number of credits selected for distance education courses shall not exceed
half of the total credits taken in a semester.
(IV). Students are limited to dropping a maximum of two courses each semester.
II. Students are required to comply with the following regulations:
(I). In accordance with the Ministry of Education’s Implementation Regulations
Regarding Distance Learning in Universities and Article 4 of the University's
Regulations for the Implementation of Distance Education, students may enroll
in distance education courses for up to one-half of the total credits required for
graduation.
(II). Undergraduate and Extension Education Bachelor program students shall not fall
below the required credits after deducting the credits from withdrawn courses.
Students shall adhere to the University’s other course selection regulations.
Students with extended study periods shall remain enrolled in at least one course
after withdrawing. Graduate students shall remain enrolled in at least one course
or a zero-credit thesis course after withdrawing.
III. The credits earned by students for selecting “Master courses” from other universities
shall not be included in the excess credits for undergraduate students.
IV. The credits earned by students for selecting “Satellite Courses” and “Master
courses” from other universities shall generally be recognized as free electives or
cross-department electives and shall be handled according to the regulations of the
student’s department.
Article 4. Students selecting “Master Courses”, “Satellite Courses”, and “Regular Courses” offered
by the University shall adhere to the University’s applicable course selection regulations.
Article 5. Any other matters not covered herein shall be handled in accordance with the relevant
regulations of the University and the Ministry of Education.
Article 6. These Guidelines shall be implemented after approval by the Academic Affairs Conference
and subsequent endorsement by the President. The same applies to any subsequent
revisions.

國立臺北大學 AI 學程聯盟選課須知(修正後條文)
本校113年10月9日第62次教務會議通過
本校114年5月22日113學年度第2學期人工智慧(AI)學分學程委員會通過修正三(四)
本校114年10月8日第64次教務會議通過修正三(四)
一、為配合教育部「臺灣大專院校人工智慧學程聯盟(TAICA)」(以下簡稱 AI 學程聯盟)
之開課選課作業，特訂定本選課須知，俾利本校學生選課並於修畢學分後，能申請
認證 AI 學程聯盟所規劃設計之學分學程。
二、AI 學程聯盟開設課程類型，說明如下：
(一)主導課程：係指由 AI 學程聯盟計畫辦公室經專家審核選擇的示範性課程，並由 AI
學程聯盟計畫辦公室指定之聯盟學校(他校或本校)所開課之課程。
(二)衛星課程：利用主導課程資源，於本校開授的課程。
(三)常規課程：本校校內開設課程與主導課程類似，可經校內相關程序認抵，並經 AI 學
程聯盟計畫辦公室複審通過之課程。
三、學生選修 AI 學程聯盟開設之他校「主導課程」，相關選課規範說明如下：
(一)不適用本校以下選課規範限制：
1.校際選課學分數不得超過該學期修習學分總數之三分之一。
2.學士班及進修學士班學生互選課程之學分數，不得超過該學期修習學分總數之三分
之一。
3.選修遠距教學課程不得超過該學期總學分數二分之一。
4.每學期僅限棄修 2 科。
(二)學生仍須遵守以下規範：
1.依據教育部「專科以上學校遠距教學實施辦法」及本校遠距教學實施辦法第四條之
規定，學生選修遠距教學課程以不超過畢業總學分數二分之一為限。
2.扣除棄修之學分數後，學士班及進修學士班學生總學分數不得低於應修學分數，且
仍應依本校其他選課相關規定辦理；延長修業年限學生棄修後仍應修習一個科目；
研究所學生棄修後仍應修習一個科目或論文(0 學分)課程。
(三)學生選修他校「主導課程」之學分數，不計入學士班學生超修學分數。
(四)學生選修「衛星課程」、他校「主導課程」，原則上採計為自由或外系選修學分，並
依學生所屬學系規定辦理。
四、學生選修本校開設之主導課程、衛星課程及常規課程，仍應依本校相關選課規定辦
理。
五、本須知若有未規定事宜，悉依本校及教育部有關法令規定辦理。
六、本須知應經教務會議通過，陳請校長核定後實施；修正時亦同。

## 國立臺北大學單獨招收身心障礙學生招生規定(105.3新訂)
### Page 1
國立臺北大學單獨招收身心障礙學生招生規定(全條文共十三條)
105年2月2日105學年度單獨招收身心障礙學生招生委員會第1次會議通過
教育部105年3月3日1050030152號函核定
第一條 本規定為辦理單獨招收身心障礙學生招生（以下簡稱本招生），依大學法第二
十四條、大學法施行細則第十九條以及「大學校院辦理單獨招收身心障礙學生
處理原則」之規定訂定之。
第二條 本招生，應依「國立臺北大學招生委員會設置辦法」組成校級招生委員會(以下
簡稱本會)，必要時得邀請相關人員列席，秉持公平、公正、公開原則辦理招生
事宜，並處理招生有關緊急事項。
各學系為辦理本招生，應設置招生工作小組，置成員若干人，其組織由學系自
訂，成員人數不得少於三人，學系主管為當然成員，並兼召集人。學系招生小
組應擬訂招生名額、考試方式、考試科目、成績比例及各項審查等有關事項，
由召集人提報本會核備。
第三條 招生簡章應詳列招生學系、招生名額、報考資格、報名方式、應繳資料、考試
方式、考試日期、評分標準、放榜公告、成績複查、報到程序、遞補規定、招
生紛爭處理程序及其他相關規定，提經本會審議通過後，招生簡章最遲應於受
理報名前二十天公告。每位學生限報考本校一學系。
第四條 報考資格為國內外公立或已立案之私立高中（職）以上學校畢業或符合報考大
學同等學力認定標準第二條規定者，並應具有下列資格之一：
一、領有身心障礙手冊或證明者。
二、經各直轄市、縣（市）政府特殊教育學生鑑定及就學輔導委員會鑑定為身
心障礙安置就學且有證明者。
持境外學歷報考者需符合相關學歷採認辦法規定。
考試日期由教育部協調後統一訂定。
第五條 各學系單獨招生名額以該學系年度核定招生名額外加百分之十為限。
第六條 辦理本考試應考量身心障礙學生之障礙情形做適性規劃。考試方式包括書面審
查、面試、術科等，儘可能摒除筆試為原則，由各學系自訂。
第七條 本招生應於放榜前決定最低錄取標準，考生成績在此標準以上且於招生名額內
之考生，列為正取生，其餘之非正取生，得列為備取生。
本招生考生成績達最低錄取標準之人數不足招生名額時，得檢具理由，提送校
級招生委員會核定後，不足額錄取，並不得列備取生。
正取生報到後，遇缺額得於招生簡章規定期限前，以備取生遞補到原核定招生
### Page 2
名額數為止。
錄取學生最後一名如有二人以上總成績相同或備取生總成績相同時，依各學系
訂定之總成績同分參酌順序擇優錄取。
遇有特殊情形需增額錄取者，由本會開會決定，並將會議紀錄連同有關證明文
件於新生註冊入學前報教育部核定。凡通過本招生入學者，於新生報到時需繳
交正式學歷(力)證明文件，否則取消其入學資格。
第八條 考生所繳之任何證明文件，經查如有偽造、變造、假借、冒用、不實者，未入
學者取消錄取資格，已入學者開除學籍並不發給與修業有關之任何證明文件，
已畢業者或退學者繳銷其學位證書或修業證明書，並負法律責任；本規定需明
列於招生簡章中。
第九條 考生如對招生事宜有疑義，應於榜單正式公告後十日內，親筆具名載明下列事
項向校級招生委員會提出書面申訴，逾期概不受理：
一、 考生姓名、報考學系、准考證號、通訊地址、通訊電話、申訴日期。
二、申訴之事實、理由及希望之救濟措施。
申訴案由校級招生委員會議決後，於受理一個月內函覆申訴當事人，必要時應
組成專案小組公正調查處理，並告知其行政救濟程序。
第十條 本招生各試務人員對於各項試務工作皆應妥慎處理並負有保密義務。
招生委員會、書面審查、面試等委員本人或其配偶、前配偶、四親等內之血親
或三親等內之姻親或曾有此關係者應考時，對於書面審查、面試等試務應自行
迴避。
本招生所有應試評分資料需妥予保存一年，其期間自放榜日起算。但依規定提
起申訴者，應保存至申訴程序結束或行政救濟程序終結時為止。
第十一條 招生作業之各項收支，依相關會計作業規定辦理。
第十二條 本規定若有未盡事宜，悉依教育部、本校學則及相關辦法之規定辦理。
第十三條 本規定經本校招生委員會通過，並報教育部核定後實施，修正時亦同。

國立臺北大學學生實習辦法
本校105年3月14日第53次行政會議通過
本校106年3月15日第55次行政會議修正通過第三、七、八條條文
本校107年3月14日第57次行政會議修正通過第三、四、五、七條條文
本校110年10月13日第72次行政會議修正通過第十條條文
本校114年3月12日第84次行政會議修正通過第三、十條條文
本校114年4月23日第59次校務會議修正通過第三、十條條文
第一條 依技術及職業教育法第十二條、專科以上學校產學合作實施辦法第六條及本
校學則第七十三條規定，為提升學生專業實務能力及保障學生實習權益，特
訂定本辦法。
第二條 本校學生實習包含：
一、專業實習課程：各教學單位得依系所發展特色及課程教學目標，針對學科專業
結合學生職涯所需技能規劃與實施校內外實習課程，使學生提早體驗職場，
建立正確工作態度，並激發學生學習及進行未來生涯發展規劃。
二、非課程型態之實習：由學生事務處職涯發展中心擔任資訊轉知窗口，提供學生
志願性校內外職場體驗媒合機會與資訊。
前項學生之實習依照實習內容及性質，分別由教務處及學生事務處等權責單
位統籌各項行政事務。
第三條 為辦理學生實習事務，設置校級學生實習委員會（以下簡稱本委員會），由
學術副校長擔任主任委員，教務長、學生事務長、國際事務長、進修暨推廣
部部主任、各學院院長為當然委員，各學院選任教師代表一人為選任委員，
學生會推舉各學院學生代表一人，校外法律學者專家一人，校外實習機構代
表三人，由主任委員簽請校長聘任之。當然委員之任期配合各該主管之任
期，選任委員、校外法律學者專家及校外實習機構代表之任期為二年，學生
會推舉各學院學生代表任期為一年。
校級學生實習委員會開會時，教學發展中心主任、職涯發展中心主任、課務
組組長、註冊組組長及進修教育組組長均應列席。
本委員會每學年以召開一次為原則，必要時得召開臨時會議；並得邀請相關
單位或人員列席報告或說明。
第四條 校級學生實習委員會之任務如下：
一、督導合作機構之評估及選定。
二、檢核及確認書面契約。
三、評估全校實習成效及督導學生申訴、爭議及意外事件之處理。
四、督導學生實習期滿前終止實習之處理。
五、督導與合作機構訂定學生個別實習計畫。
六、督導實習輔導訪視之落實。
七、其他學生權益保障相關事項。
### Page 3
第五條 實習如為校外實習時，其實施方式、實習場所、師資、學分採計、輔導及其
他學生校外實習權益保障相關規定，除法令另有規定外，由各執行單位定
之。
各執行單位應設立院、系、所、中心或學位學程學生實習委員會，協助校外
實習課程之規劃與實施，以確保學生校外實習權益。
前項院、系、所、中心或學位學程學生實習委員會之組成，由各執行單位定
之，其任務如下：
一、整體規劃及推動校外實習課程。
二、確認合作機構之評估結果及選定。
三、擬訂書面契約及學生個別實習計畫。
四、協調、處理學生申訴、爭議及意外事件。
五、處理學生實習期滿前之終止實習。
六、追蹤處理及檢討學生實習輔導訪視結果。
七、其他學生權益保障相關事項。
第六條 學生校外實習機構由各執行單位確實進行篩選與評估，如為學生推薦之校外實
習機構，亦應經各執行單位核准後始得進行實習。
第七條 各執行單位與實習機構合作應簽訂合作書面契約，載明雙方權利義務以維護學
生權益，包含：
一、合作機構依學生個別實習計畫提供學生相關實務訓練，並與學校指派之專責輔
導教師共同輔導學生。
二、合作機構負責學生實習前之安全講習、實習場所安全防護設備之配置及相關安
全措施之規劃。
三、為實習學生投保相關保險。
四、明定實習時間（每日學習時間、請假或例假規定）、合約期限、實習內容、實
習獎學金或薪資之給付、膳宿及交通、成績評核基準等項目。
五、合作機構與實習學生發生爭議時之協調及處理方式。
六、學生實習期滿前終止或解除之條件及程序。
學生實習期間於合作機構有從事學習訓練以外之勞務提供或工作事實者，所定
之書面契約應依勞動基準法規定辦理。
第八條 教育實習課程另依師資培育法及相關規定辦理。
社會工作實習課程另依專門職業及技術人員高等考試社會工作師考試規則等
相關規定辦理。
第九條 學生自主校外實習不適用本辦法。
第十條 本辦法應經校務會議通過，陳請校長核定後實施；修正時亦同。

第一條 本校為提昇教學品質，肯定及表揚教師在教學上之卓越貢獻，特依
大學法第 21 條訂定本辦法。
第二條 本辦法所稱教師係指在本校連續任教一學年以上之專任教授、副教
授、助理教授及講師（含編制外約聘專任教學人員）。
前項任教年資採計至遴選前一學年七月底止。前項任教年資採計至
遴選前一學年七月底止。
第二條之ㄧ 教師接受教學意見調查之當學年度所開課程，如有下列情形之
ㄧ者，排除受獎：
一、依國立臺北大學教師授課時數及超支鐘點費核計辦法核計
結果，其基本授課時數不足者。
二、依國立臺北大學教師授課時數及超支鐘點費核計辦法核計結
果，雖基本授課時數已滿足，但其被採計為基本授課時數的
科目當中，包含有國立臺北大學學生選課辦法第十六條第
### Page 2
二項之情事者。
第三條 每學年教學優良教師及教學特優教師之名額合計，以受評當學年第
一學期之全體專任教師人數百分之四(無條件捨去至整數)為上限。
各學院之獲獎名額，依該學院教師人數比例分配（無條件捨去至小
數點後二位）。若依比例計算後，各學院分配之獲獎名額總和低於
本校獲獎總名額，則依小數部分最高之學院依序補足。
小數部分採逐年累計後使用，惟若學院獲補名額超出其當年度應得
比例，則該超額部分於次年扣回。
體育室、通識教育中心與語言中心併入人文學院計算教師人數；資
訊中心併入電機資訊學院計算。
符合本辦法第四條各院前百分之十之教師，但未獲選教學優良教師
者，另頒發教學績優教師獎。
累計三學年曾獲選為教學優良教師者，第三學年獲獎時，改頒教學
特優教師獎。教學績優教師、教學優良教師及教學特優教師獲獎
人，由學校致贈表揚狀及獎金；教學績優教師獎金新臺幣一萬元至
三萬元，教學優良教師獎金新臺幣三萬元至五萬元，教學特優教師
獎金新臺幣六萬元至八萬元。
第四條 教學優良教師之獎勵，每學年辦理一次，其作業細則另訂之，並
依下列階段進行：
一、第一階段：由學校統一製作問卷，以修習學士班、進修學士
班、碩士班、碩士在職專班及博士班課程之學生所做之全學年教
學意見調查及教學優良教師問卷調查之結果為依據。
二、第二階段：
(一)依第一階段計算結果排序，提出各院專任教師(含編制外約聘
專任教學人員)前百分之十之名單（小數點無條件進位），送請
各該學院院長召集所屬副院長、系(所、學位學程)主管組成院
級遴選委員會。
體育室、通識教育中心與語言中心之教師，納入人文學院一起排
名與審議。人文學院上開前百分之十之名單，若出現體育室、通識
教育中心或語言中心之教師時，人文學院召集所屬系、所、中心主
管之聯席會議，須增加邀請上開名單中之教師所屬單位主管一起出
席，參加審議。資訊中心之教師納入電機資訊學院一起排名與審
議。電機資訊學院上開前百分之十之名單，若出現資訊中心之教師
時， 電機學院召集所屬系、所、中心主管之院級遴選委員會時，
須增加邀請上開名單中之教師所屬單位主管一起出席，參加審
議。
(二)院級遴選委員會就第一款全學年教學意見調查與教學優良教
師問卷調查，以及教學相關項目，開會審議並排序推薦教學優
良教師候選人。
三、第三階段：由學校組成校級遴選委員會，按第三條規定之名
### Page 3
額決定教學優良教師、教學特優教師，與教學績優教師受獎名
單。
候選教師不得擔任遴選委員。
第五條 教學優良教師校級遴選委員會，由校長聘請教務長及各學院教師一
名為委員組成之， 由教務長擔任召集人，委員會議須有二分之一
以上委員親自出席始得開議。
第六條 獲獎為教學特優教師者，在次一學年暫停被推薦為教學優良教師，
之後得再被推薦，且教學優良獲獎次數重新起算。
第七條 前條累計三學年教學優良獎勵採計年度，自 96 學年度開始起算。
第八條 教學優良教師遴選所需獎金及經費支出，由校務基金自籌收入或教
育部高等教育深耕計畫補助款支應之；其經費之給與應在不造成學
校實質虧損及國庫負擔之前提下支給。
第八條之一 本校得邀請獲得教學優良教師獎或教學特優教師獎之教師，參
與教學精進相關研習活動或分享教學經驗與心得。
第九條 本辦法應經校務基金管理委員會決議提請校務會議通過後實施，修正
時亦同。

### Page 3
國立臺北大學雲端國際專業證照補助辦法
110年7月21日經校長核定
113年3月25日經校長核定修正
第一條 本校為鼓勵教師提升雲端教學創新能力及培養學生實務能力，激勵教師及學生取得
雲端國際專業證照，特訂定「國立臺北大學雲端國際專業證照補助辦法」（以下簡
稱本辦法）。
第二條 本辦法補助內容如下：
一、補助對象為本校專任教師（含編制外約聘專任教學人員）及在學學生，取得雲
端國際專業證照，得申請補助部分報名費，補助證照種類依教務處教學發展中
心最新公告為主。
二、依「大陸地區人民來臺就讀專科以上學校辦法」第14條規定，大陸地區學生不
得申請。
三、補助名額將視當年度經費狀況而定。
第三條 本辦法補助金額如下：
一、補助金額依據證照類別分為3級。
第1級：取得專家級證照者，可申請補助金新臺幣3,000元整。
第2級：取得助理級證照者，可申請補助金新臺幣2,000元整。
第3級：取得基礎級證照者，可申請補助金新臺幣1,200元整。
二、以上各級證照補助之申請，每人在學期間以每級別各1次為限。
三、已申請其他校內外相同級別證照之獎勵或補助者，不得重複申請。若重複申請
補助經查屬實者，不予補助，已補助者應予追回。
第四條 申請人應於取得證照後，依下列規定時間，備齊申請文件向教務處教學發展中心申
請。資料不齊、逾期之申請案件不予受理。
一、當年度通過證照考試者，最遲應於同年度11月30日前提出申請。
二、畢業生應於畢業離校前2週提出申請。例如：於6月30日辦理離校，應於6月15日
前申請，以此類推。
第五條 本辦法所需經費，由教育部高教深耕計畫經費支應。
第六條 本辦法經陳請校長核定後發布施行，修正時亦同。
