第 一 條 依據本校學則第三十六條規定訂定本辦法。
第 二 條 具下列身分之學生得申請抵免學分，各種身分申請抵免學分以一次為原則：
一、轉系（所）生。
二、轉學生。
三、曾在本校或他校肄、畢業，重新取得學籍之新生。
四、取得學籍之選讀生。
五、先修讀學分，後考取修讀學位者。
六、依本校學生出國選修課程實施辦法至外國大學選修課程者。
七、經學術交流至教育部認可名冊所列大陸、港、澳地區學校修讀學分者。
八、依照修課規定准許於入學前先修讀學分者。
### Page 3
九、跨校或校內雙重學籍學生。
雙主修或輔系或教育學程之學生，除依前項規定辦理外，得於修業年限之最後一學
年再申請辦理一次。
抵免學分應於入（轉）學、轉系、申請修習輔系或雙主修當學年加退選結束前辦理
完成。
第 三 條 抵免學分之原則規定如下：
一、科目名稱及內容相同者。
二、科目名稱不同而內容相同者。
三、科目名稱及內容不同而性質相同者。
四、擬抵免之學分所屬之學位，應高於或相當於被抵免之學分所屬之學位。
五、擬申請抵免之課程與學分(校訂共同必修除外)不得重複採計於其他學位。
於專科學校（五專限四、五年級）修習成績及格之科目學分，得申請抵免學士學位
之科目學分，不適用前項第四款、第五款之規定。
本校法律學系碩士班法律專業組學生曾修習本校學士班科目學分，成績達七十分以
上且未列入學士班畢業學分者，得就與學士班合班授課且科目名稱及內容相同之碩
士學位科目學分申請抵免，不適用第一項第四款之規定。
第 四 條 抵免學分範圍如下：
一、本系必、選修學分。
二、轉系學分。
三、雙主修學分。
四、輔系學分。
五、學程學分。
六、通識學分之抵免，限語文通識依開課單位相關規定辦理；向度通識依本校通識教
育中心抵免學分辦法辦理。
七、限本校教學單位科目規劃表內科目。
轉系生已在本校修習之校定共同必、選修科目，直接承認，不須辦理抵免。惟向
度通識畢業學分之認定依畢業時所屬學系及雙主修學系認定之。
第 五 條 不同學分互抵後之處理規定如下：
一、以多抵少者，抵免後以較少學分登記。
二、以少抵多不足學分無法補修者，不得抵免；唯可抵免部分學分者，抵免後應補修
不足學分。
第 六 條 第二條所列各類學生抵免學分多寡、範圍與轉（編）入年級配合規定如下：
一、轉系生轉入二年級者，其抵免學分總數以五十學分為限；轉入三年級者，其抵免
學分總數以一百學分為限，但降轉生得由各學系認定酌予增加抵免學分數。自轉
入年級起，每學期至少應修學分數不得低於下限規定。又轉入三年級者抵免相
當學分後，須於修業年限（不包括延長年限）依照學期限修學分規定而可修畢
轉入學系最低畢業學分；否則，應降級轉入二年級。
二、轉學生比照前款規定辦理。
三、重新取得本校學籍之新生，其已修習及格之有關科目與學分，准予申請抵免，
但專業科目（除校訂共同必修及通識教育科目以外之科目）以最近四年內修讀
者為限。其已抵免科目之學分達一定標準者，得提高編級，但至少須修業一年， 並
依照學期限修學分數修習始可畢業。
四、經重考入學各該學系一年級時，已抵免部份之原就讀學校修習科目學分，如再行
轉入他系就讀，其原就讀學校修習科目學分應經重新認定抵免，但以五十學分
為上限。
五、除已列入原畢業學分者外，依照法令准許先修讀較高階學位之學分，其後入學修
### Page 4
讀該較高階學位（含選讀生）之新生，其於本校最近四年內已修讀符合較高階
學位及格規定之有關科目與學分，准予申請抵免，學士班與進修學士班新生抵
免學分總數以五十學分為限，碩博士班學生以就讀系（所）規定畢業學分數二
分之一為限，但畢業學分數低於三十六學分者，則以十八學分為限。
六、曾於本校修讀博士學位而未取得學位者，於再次考入博士班入學前十年內所修讀
之博士班課程學分可申請抵免，得不受第三款之限制。
七、曾於本校修讀碩士學位而未取得學位者，於再次考入碩士班入學前十年內所修讀
之碩士班課程學分可申請抵免，得不受第三款之限制。
八、重新取得學籍或依照法令規定先修讀學分後考取修讀學位之碩士在職專班學生，
於入學前十年內所修讀之碩士學分可申請抵免，得不受第三款、第五款四年內修讀
之限制，但各系所若有更嚴格之規定，從其規定。
九、學士班與進修學士班新生抵免學分總數達四十學分者，得編入二年級；抵免學分
總數達八十學分者，得編入三年級。申請提高編級以一次為限且應於辦理學分
抵免時一併提出申請，經核准後不得申請變更或撤銷。
十、學生以修習推廣教育學分班取得學分辦理抵免後，在校修業不得少於該學制修業期限
及畢業應修學分數二分之一，且不得少於一年。學生以遠距教學方式取得之推廣教
育學分申請學分抵免，其科目學分數已超過畢業總學分數之三分之一者，該學系
所應專案報請教務處，由教務處造冊報教育部備查。
十一、雙重學籍學生入學本校就讀前，於他校修習之學分，得依本辦法第二條第一項
第三款申請抵免；於本校就讀後再入學他校就讀或同時入學就讀，他校修習學
分不予抵免。在本校擁有雙重學籍者抵免比照辦理，惟畢業時學分需符合第三
條第一項第五款規定。
第 七 條 抵免學分總數確定後，依前條之規定由教務處編入適當年級，並將編班情形在新
生名冊上註記。科目學分經確定相互抵免並經登錄後，不得更換抵免其他科目學
分。
第 八 條 學分之抵免，應依下列規定辦理：
一、各系（所、室、中心）應公告學分抵免流程。學生應檢具抵免學分申請書及歷
年成績表，於規定時間內向各系（所、室、中心）提出抵免學分申請。
二、抵免學分之審核，各系（所、室、中心）應依本辦法自行訂定規則辦理，並報
教務單位備查。該學分抵免之審核，由學生所屬系所主管或任課教師核實認定， 或
舉行考試通過後，報請教務單位覆核。
第 九 條 凡曾在符合教育部採認規定之外國大學及教育部認可名冊所列大陸、港、澳地區學校修
讀之科目、學分，依本辦法有關規定辦理抵免。
第 十 條 本辦法應經教務會議通過，陳請校長核定後實施；修正時亦同。

## 國立臺北大學教務會議議事規則(108.03新訂)
### Page 1
國立臺北大學教務會議議事規則
108年3月27日第51次教務會議延續會通過
第一條 國立臺北大學（以下簡稱本校）為順利進行教務會議（以下簡稱
本會議），特訂定本議事規則。
第二條 本會議之提案包括下列事項：
一、校長交議事項。
二、各院、系、所、學位學程經院、系、所、學位學程務會議通
過之提議事項。
三、教務會議代表所屬行政單位之提議事項。
四、教務會議代表三人以上連署之提議事項。
五、經校級課程委員會通過之提議事項。
六、教務處對於其他有關教務事項之提議
前項提案須以書面為之。
第三條 本會議應有會議代表過半人數以上出席，始得開議。
本會議代表應親自出席會議，因故不能出席會議時，得以委託書
授權代理人代為出席。
第四條 本會議代表對於同一提案之發言，以每人不超過三次、每次不超
過三分鐘為限；但對於提案之說明、質疑之應答、事實資料之補
充、工作或重要事項之報告及情況特殊經主席同意者，不在此限。
第五條 本會議代表之發言應先取得主席許可，如二人以上同時請求發言
時，其先後次序由主席決定之。
本會議主席有維持議場秩序之權責，會議代表如有違反本議事規
則或有其他妨礙議場秩序之行為，主席得禁止其發言。
第六條 本會議主席對提案之討論，得於適當時機提請表決。表決方式由
主席酌情採用舉手、投票之方式。
列席人員無表決權。
第七條 臨時動議應有在場會議代表三人以上附議始可提出；並經在場會
議代表過半人數同意，始可進行提案討論。
第八條 本會議僅作決議紀錄，所有發言均應錄音存檔備查。
前項錄音，由教務處辦理。
本會議出、列席人員不得擅自於議場內錄音、錄影或攝影，違反
者經主席制止仍不聽從，主席得強制其離開議場。
第九條 本議事規則未盡事宜，準用內政部公布施行之會議規範辦理。
第十條 本議事規則經教務會議通過後施行，修訂時亦同。

114年3月19日本校第63次教務會議修正通過第二條條文
一、本校為全面性推動大學程式設計教育，提升學生邏輯運算思維、運用資訊科技能力，特
訂定「國立臺北大學程式設計相關課程審議小組設置要點」（以下簡稱本要點）。
二、程式設計相關課程審議小組（以下簡稱本小組），設置委員共十二名，以教務長為主任
委員兼召集人，課務組組長、教學發展中心主任為當然委員，其餘委員由法律學院、商

表一人、資訊中心選任代表一人、學生自治會推舉學生代表一名；主任委員亦得視需要
邀請校外學者專家列席。
本小組委員均為無給職，校外委員得支給出席費及交通費。
三、本小組任務如下：
（一）推動本校程式設計教育目標、商議重要決策及原則，並檢視實施成效。
（二）校外程式設計相關證照之審議。
（三）校內程式設計相關課程之審議。
四、本小組開會時須有二分之一(含)以上委員出席方得開會，非經出席委員二分之一(含)以
上同意，不得決議。
五、本小組得依任務需求進行分組，每學期以開會一次為原則，必要時得召開臨時會議，並
視需要邀請相關單位人員列席。
六、本要點經教務會議通過，陳請校長核定後實施，修正時亦同。

## 國立臺北大學實習課程開設原則(110.12修訂)
### Page 1
國立臺北大學實習課程開設原則
修正對照表
修正條文 現行條文 說明
第七條 第七條 依秘書室 110 年 3 月 23
本原則應經校級學生實 本原則經校級學生實習 日 北 大 秘 字 第
習委員會通過，陳請校長 委員會通過後實施，修正 1109500046號函辦理。

### Page 2
國立臺北大學實習課程開設原則
109.05.27 108學年度校級學生實習委員會議通過
110.12.15 110學年度校級學生實習委員會議修正通過第七條條文
一.實習課程定義：係指系（所、學程、中心等）依其課程屬性自訂學分數，並依其課程規
劃對應專業能力所進行之職場屬性實務學習課程，不含見習、實驗及實作訓練課程。
二.實習課程開設，每一學分實習時數至多 80小時，應依下列方式辦理：
(一)非全時實習課程：學生部分時間於校內外實習單位實習之課程，實習時數由開課單
位決定之，得於寒假、暑期或學期期間進行。
(二)全時實習課程：
1.全時學期實習課程：開課單位於學期間，得開設 9學分以上之整學期實習課程， 學
生全學期全部時間均在校外機構實習，實習期間至少為期 4.5個月。
2.全時學年實習課程：開課單位得開設 18 學分以上之全學年實習課程，學生學年全
部時間均在校外機構實習，實習期間至少為期 9 個月以上。 課程名稱及學分數應
明定於實習契約。
三.實習課程學分費：
(一)非全時實習課程：
1.於學期/學年開課，依學雜費收費標準收費。
2.於暑期開課，依暑期開班授課辦法規定辦理。
(二)僅修習「全時實習課程」者，收學費全部、雜費五分之四。
四.實習課程教師鐘點費核計：悉依「國立臺北大學教師授課時數及超支鐘點費核計辦法」
相關條文辦理。
各類課程若有教師合授課程，合授教師應均分授課時數，或各自協調授課時數；若為同
時分組授課，則每組學生人數應符合開課人數標準，始得不均分授課時數。
五.師資培育之教育實習及教學實習課程，因配合教育部規定，不適用本原則實習課程定義。
專門職業及技術人員考試之實習課程，因配合考試院規定，不適用本原則實習課程 學
分數與實習時數之規定。惟教師鐘點時數核計仍須依本校教師授課時數及超支鐘點費
核計辦法規定辦理。
六.本原則未盡事宜，依本校學生實習辦法及教育部相關法規辦理。
七.本原則應經校級學生實習委員會通過，陳請校長核定後實施，修正時亦同。

## 國立臺北大學數位科技融入教學課程補助要點(113.03新訂)
### Page 1
國立臺北大學數位科技融入教學課程補助要點
本校 113年3月20日第61次教務會議通過
一、本校為鼓勵教師將數位科技融入教學，並開設相關課程，教務處教學發展中心（以下簡
稱本中心）特訂定「國立臺北大學數位科技融入教學課程補助要點」（以下簡稱本要點）。
二、本要點所稱數位科技融入教學課程如下：
(一) 將擴增實境(AR)、虛擬實境(VR)、磨課師(MOOCs)、人工智慧(AI)、雲端運算(Cloud
Computing)、開放教育資源(OER)等數位科技導入教學之課程。
(二) 申請教育部遠距教學數位學習課程認證之課程。
三、本要點補助對象為於本校開設課程之專兼任教師，補助課程不含博士班、進修學士班、
碩士在職專班。
四、本要點補助期間以每學期初本中心公告為主。
五、本要點補助原則如下：
(一) 每位教師每學期以申請 1門為限，每門課以補助 2次為限。
(二) 每門課至多補助10,000 元。
(三) 受補助之課程不得重複接受本中心相關計畫補助。
六、由本中心召開審查會議，審查原則如下：
(一) 課程整體規劃：含課程目標、課程架構、課程內容、導入數位科技課程類型安排、
預期教學成效等，至少導入 4週。
(二) 課程可移轉性：是否可供其他課程複製使用。
(三) 前期課程成果及教學評鑑（初次申請免審）。
(四) 經費編列合理性。
七、受本要點補助之課程授課教師權利與義務如下：
(一) 須於當學期繳交教學案例成果（教學紀錄至少 4 週）電子檔案 1 份至本中心，報告
繳交之狀況，將列入未來計畫申請之評核依據。
(二) 須配合本中心辦理各項推廣事宜（如成果展示、心得發表、示範教學或經驗分享等），
並提供相關資料。
(三) 課程內容不得侵害他人之智慧財產權及其他權利。如有涉及使用智慧財產權之糾紛
或任何權利之侵害時，由申請教師自負法律責任。
(四) 依本要點補助之課程成果，同意國立臺北大學及所屬機關，以任何方式行使著作財
產權，得不限地域、時間、方式，無償利用及重製。
八、補助所需經費由教育部高教深耕計畫經費及大學聯盟深化數位學習推展與創新應用計畫
補助經費支應，補助額度及件數視本中心當年度經費調整。
九、本要點經教務會議通過後實施，修正時亦同。

## National Taipei University Guidelines for the Employment of Industry Experts in Co-teaching (110.10 revised)
### Page 1
National Taipei University Guidelines for the Employment of Industry Experts
in Co-teaching
In case of any discrepancy or inconsistency between the English and Chinese versions of the
regulations, the Chinese version shall take precedence.
Article 1. National Taipei University (hereinafter referred to as “the University”) has established
these guidelines to enhance the quality of curriculum instruction and strengthen the
integration of theoretical and practical knowledge by incorporating industry experts
(hereinafter referred to as “industry experts”) into teaching, in accordance with the Ministry
of Education’s "Regulations for the Employment of Industry Experts in Collaborative
Teaching at Colleges and Higher Education Institutions.”
Article 2. The experts specified in these guidelines shall meet one of the following qualifications:
I. Experts who have graduated from domestic or foreign colleges or higher educational
institutions, possess more than five years of practical professional work experience
directly related to the teaching domain, and have demonstrated outstanding
performance.
II. Experts who have not graduated from domestic or foreign colleges or higher
educational institutions but possess over ten years of professional work experience
directly related to the teaching domain and have demonstrated outstanding
performance.
III. A former coach, referee, or athlete who has participated in national-level or higher
professional competitions.
IV. The candidate’s professional practical experience, as recognized by the recruiting unit,
meets the requirements for professional practice courses and renders them fully
qualified for this position.
Article 3. Individuals meeting any of the criteria specified in Article 2, Section 3 of the “Regulations
for the Employment of Industry Experts in Collaborative Teaching at Colleges and
Universities” shall not be eligible for employment as industry experts. The procedures
outlined in Article 2, Section 4 shall be followed accordingly.
### Page 2
Article 4. The guidelines for the selection and review mechanism of industry expert recruitment are
as follows:
I. The employment period: In line with the semester system, employment is on a per-
semester basis; however, for full-year courses, it may be on an annual basis.
II. Faculty members from various teaching units who wish to apply for industry expert
co-teaching must complete the review and hiring process one semester before the
actual teaching begins.
III. The review procedure: If an industry expert’s participation in collaborative teaching
for a course in a full semester involves 9 sessions or more but fewer than 12 sessions,
the appointing unit will prepare the “Application Form for Recruiting Industry Experts
in Collaborative Teaching” and the “Curriculum Vitae of the Industry Expert for
Collaborative Teaching.” These documents will be submitted to the departmental
curriculum committee for evaluation and approval. If participation exceeds 12
sessions per course in a full semester, the documents will be submitted for review and
approval by both the departmental and college-level curriculum committees. After
approval, the appointment contract will be signed by the respective teaching units and
issued following endorsement by the University President.
Article 5. The Principles of Industry Expert Collaborative Teaching:
I. Target Audience for Implementation: Industry experts with practical field experience
may be invited to participate in collaborative teaching or guidance in courses offered
or supervised by the university's full-time (or adjunct) faculty members, including
those in the Undergraduate and Extension Education Bachelor Programs.
II. Collaborative Teaching Model: The collaborative teaching referred to in these
guidelines involves the primary instruction of the course being conducted by the
designated full-time faculty member, with supplementary practical teaching provided
by industry experts. The full-time faculty member must be present throughout the
sessions led by the industry expert. The industry expert's responsibilities primarily
include collaborating in practical course instruction, such as joint curriculum planning,
developing case materials, guiding students in practical projects, conducting case
### Page 3
studies (including assessment and analysis), participating in external competitions,
certification examinations, and exhibitions, among other duties.
III. The Number of Collaborative Teaching Courses: Each teaching unit is limited to
offering industry expert collaborative teaching in up to one-third of the total courses
for a semester. Additionally, each industry expert is limited to collaboratively
teaching a maximum of two courses per semester. A maximum of two industry
experts may collaborate on the same course.
Article 6. For the originally scheduled teaching by full-time faculty members, their teaching hours
and hourly rates shall be calculated based on the original course arrangement. The hourly
rates for industry experts can be determined according to the university's standard for dual-
teacher teaching remuneration. Additionally, these experts may receive reimbursement for
round-trip transportation expenses, which will be verified and reimbursed according to the
regulations for domestic business travel expenses. The costs may be covered by relevant
subsidy programs or self-raised funds by the appointing units.
Article 7. The matters not covered in these guidelines shall be handled in accordance with relevant
regulations, such as the Ministry of Education's “Regulations for the Employment of
Industry Experts in Collaborative Teaching in Colleges.”
Article 8. These guidelines should be implemented after approval by the administrative meeting and
subsequent endorsement by the President. The same procedure applies to any subsequent
revisions.

## National Taipei University Academic Regulations
### Page 1
National Taipei University Academic Regulations
Chapter 1 General Provisions
In case of any discrepancy or inconsistency between the English and Chinese versions of the
regulations, the Chinese version shall take precedence.
Article 1. National Taipei University (NTPU or “the University”) hereby formulates these Academic
Regulations (the “Regulations”) in accordance with the University Act, the Enforcement
Rules of the University Act, and other relevant laws and regulations, while also taking into
account the specific needs of the University.
Article 2. The University shall regulate student status, grades, degree conferral, and other related
matters in accordance with these Regulations. Specific implementation measures may be
stipulated separately.
Flexible study measures will be established separately for male students in the
Undergraduate program and Extension Education Bachelor program who were born in or
after 1994 and are subject to compulsory military service starting from January 1, 2024.
These measures will be submitted to the Ministry of Education for record.
Article 3. The University admits new students to each department (degree program, institute) every
academic year. Undergraduate program and Extension Education Bachelor programs may
also admit transfer students into the sophomore and junior years. The enrollment guidelines
shall be determined separately.
Article 4. The University shall admit students in accordance with the provisions of the law and
relevant regulations. This includes overseas Chinese students, Mainland Chinese students,
international students, students holding a disability ID card, distinguished athletes, students
from outlying islands admitted through recommendation, and screened applicants.
Article 5. The University Academic Regulations apply to all students except where specific
exceptions are provided, in addition to the educational laws and related University
regulations and procedures.
Chapter 2 Admissions
Article 6. Graduates of public or registered private high schools, or equivalent education level
schools, or foreign students approved by the Ministry of Education (MOE) who pass the
### Page 2
University’s entrance examination may enroll in the first year of the University's
undergraduate program or Extension Education Bachelor program.
The admission of Extension Education Bachelor Program students shall be handled in
accordance with the Enrollment Guidelines.
Admissions to the post-baccalaureate program shall be conducted in accordance with the
MOE's Guidelines for Higher Education Institutions Offering Expertise Development
Courses and the Admissions Guidelines.
Article 7. Applicants who have graduated with a bachelor’s or equivalent academic degree from a
domestic university or college recognized and authorized by the MOE, or from a foreign
university or college recognized by the MOE, and who have been admitted to NTPU
through admissions, or foreign students recognized by the MOE, may be admitted to an
NTPU master’s degree program upon passing the admissions screening and test.
Applicants who have graduated with a master’s or equivalent academic degree from a
domestic university or college recognized and authorized by the MOE, or who have
graduated from a foreign university or college recognized by the MOE, and who have been
admitted to NTPU, or foreign students recognized by the MOE, may be admitted to an
NTPU doctoral degree program if they have passed the admissions screening and test for
the doctoral degree program.
The regulations for the direct-entry doctoral degree program shall be specified separately
in the University's doctoral program regulations and related examination rules.
Article 8. The quota for undergraduate students in each department and degree program, except for
first-year and graduating students, may be filled with transfer students through transfer
examinations if there are vacancies. The vacancies mentioned above shall not include those
arising from reserved admission qualifications or leaves of absence. After the transfer
student admissions process, the total number of students shall not exceed the quota
designated by the MOE for each department. Matters related to transfer examinations will
be specified separately in the enrollment guidelines.
Article 9. An act of cheating on the entrance examination that is proven to be true by the University
or confirmed by a final and binding conviction shall result in the deprivation of admission
### Page 3
status.
Those who are granted admission to NTPU as new or transfer students shall complete their
enrollment on the designated registration date. Those who fail to do so shall be disqualified
from admission. Students who are unable to enroll on time due to pregnancy, labor, raising
children aged 3 or younger, illness, or other major incidents may apply to retain their
admission status by submitting an application with the relevant supporting documents
(health certificates issued by a regional hospital or medical center under the National
Health Insurance Administration are required) to the Academic Affairs Office before the
registration deadline. Admission status can be preserved for up to 1 year, except for high
school graduates participating in the Youth Education and Employment Savings Accounts
Program, for whom the status can be preserved for up to 3 years without any fees.
Students who are allowed to retain their admission status shall follow the registration
procedures outlined for new students when enrolling in the following academic year.
Article 10. Freshmen and transfer students are required to submit their graduation certificates,
equivalent academic credentials, transfer documentation, and other necessary documents
for enrollment approval. Those with valid reasons may apply for deferred submission in
advance, subject to approval by the university. However, they must complete the
submission within the specified period; failure to do so will result in the revocation of their
admission status.
Article 11. Freshmen or transfer students who have submitted documentation that is found to be
the product of fabrication, impersonation, forgery, or falsification shall be expelled.
Students expelled from the University will not be issued any certification document for
proof of enrollment or study, and their parents or guardians shall be notified. If a student’s
submission of fraudulent documents is discovered after graduation, the University shall
revoke the student's degree and announce the revocation of their status as a graduate of the
University.
Chapter 3 Courses and Credits
Article 12. The University employs an academic-year-based credit system, with undergraduate
programs requiring four years of study. The Extension Education Bachelor program
### Page 4
recruits students into various departments and degree programs, including the Department
of Law, the Department of Business Administration, the Department of Finance and
Cooperative Management, the Department of Statistics (suspended since the 2015
academic year), the Department of Public Administration and Policy, the Department of
Public Finance, the Department of Real Estate and Built Environment, the Department of
Economics, the Department of Social Work, and the Bachelor's Degree Program in Digital
Marketing at the College of Business. The study period for each department and degree
program is as follows:
I. The study period is limited to four years for students enrolled from the academic
year 2006 onwards in the Department of Statistics (suspended since the 2015
academic year), the Department of Finance and Cooperative Management, the
Department of Economics, and the Department of Public Administration and
Policy. For students enrolled before the 2005 academic year (inclusive), the study
period is limited to five years.
II. The study period is limited to four years for students enrolled from the academic
year 2007 onwards in the Extension Education Bachelor Program of the
Department of Real Estate and Built Environment. For students enrolled before
the 2006 academic year (inclusive), the study period is limited to five years.
III. The study period is limited to four years for students enrolled from the academic
year 2008 onwards in the Extension Education Bachelor Program of the
Department of Public Finance. For students enrolled before the 2007 academic
year (inclusive), the study period is limited to five years.
IV. The study period is limited to four years for students enrolled from the academic
year 2010 onwards in the Extension Education Bachelor Program of the
Department of Law. For students enrolled before the 2009 academic year
(inclusive), the study period is limited to five years.
V. The study period is limited to four years for students enrolled from the academic
year 2011 onwards in the Extension Education Bachelor Program of the
Department of Business Administration. For students enrolled before the 2010
### Page 5
academic year (inclusive), the study period is limited to five years.
VI. The study period is limited to four years for students enrolled from the academic
year 2015 onwards in the Extension Education Bachelor Program of the
Department of Social Work. For students enrolled before the 2014 academic year
(inclusive), the study period is limited to five years.
VII. The study period is limited to four years for freshmen enrolled from the academic
year 2015 onwards in the Extension Education Bachelor Program of Digital
Marketing.
Students who have not earned the number of credits required by their respective
departments or degree programs within the prescribed period of study may apply for an
extension of study, limited to a maximum of two years. However, for students pursuing
double majors, after the two-year extension has ended, if they do not wish to drop the double
major, they may apply for an additional extension of one year. Students who meet
graduation requirements and are approved for overseas study during the period of study
may have their study period extended by up to two years for the duration of their studies
abroad. To graduate, students must complete at least 128 credits, excluding military
education and physical education, and meet any additional credit requirements specified by
their departments or degree programs, up to a limit of 20 additional credits.
Undergraduate students who have previously graduated from a school in a foreign country,
Hong Kong, or Macao, where the graduation level is academically equivalent to the second
year of a senior high school in Taiwan, shall be required to complete at least 12 additional
credits beyond the minimum total credit requirement specified in paragraph 1. The
additional credits and courses shall be determined by the students' respective department or
degree program.
For departments and degree programs with a study period of more than five years due to
practical requirements, the number of credits to be taken shall be specified separately.
Students with disability identification, or those who are certified as having a handicap status
eligible for a higher education placement program by a special education committee under
the authority of the local government, shall be allowed a four-year maximum study
### Page 6
extension, depending on related physical conditions and academic requirements.
Master’s students are required to complete at least 24 credits; doctoral students are required
to complete at least 18 credits; and students in a direct entry doctoral degree program are
required to complete at least 30 credits. Thesis and dissertation credits shall be counted
separately.
Students who are pregnant, in labor, or raising one or more children aged three or younger
may request an extension of study, which shall be submitted for approval on a case by case
basis.
The post-baccalaureate program has a program length requirement of 1 to 4 years, and
extensions are not permitted. The graduation credit requirement for the post-baccalaureate
program shall be the same as the graduation credit requirement for the respective
department’s professional courses, or at least 48 credits of professional courses. Students
who obtain a certificate of professional course credits after graduating from college may
apply for credit transfer in accordance with the Regulations Governing the Implementation
of Educational Promotion Measures for Higher Education Institutes, the Regulations
Governing the Provision of Vocational Continuing Education by Junior Colleges and
Institutions of Higher Education, and the Regulations Governing the Provision of
Vocational Continuing Education and Evaluation by Vocational Training Institutions. After
applying for credit transfer, the number of credits earned from courses that the student
subsequently takes may not be fewer than 12 credits.
Article 13. Undergraduate students and students in the Extension Education Bachelor Program
who have outstanding grades and have completed all required courses and credits within
one semester or one academic year before the end of the stipulated study period, and who
meet the graduation requirements specified in Article 72, Paragraph 1 of the Academic
Regulations, may apply for early graduation at the Academic Affairs Office or the
Extension Education Center. If approved by the director of their respective department or
academic program, the dean of the college, and the dean of Academic Affairs or the director
of the Extension Education Center, they may be granted early graduation by one semester
or one academic year.
### Page 7
Students applying under the preceding paragraph are required to meet the following criteria
for graduation:
I. Students who maintain an overall grade point average (GPA) in the top 20% of
their class (applicable to both departments and degree programs). Those with
internship requirements must complete the internship.
II. The academic average for early graduation should be 80 or above.
The department may establish more stringent standards based on the preceding
regulations.
Junior transfer students and students who are placed in a junior class or above
upon admission to the University are not eligible to apply for early graduation.
Article 14. After the respective Curriculum Committees establish the course titles, credit hours,
and year levels for university-wide and college-wide required and elective courses, as well
as for department-level (degree programs, institutes, offices, and centers) professional
required and elective courses, these details shall be submitted to the university-level
Curriculum Committee for review and approval.
Article 15. Credit points for the courses offered by the departments (degree program, institute,
offices , centers) are calculated based on the number of lecture hours. 1 credit shall be
awarded for every 18 lecture hours or for every 36 to 54 hours in an internship or laboratory
session per semester; the maximum duration of each course is 16 weeks.
Departments (degree programs, institutes, divisions, and centers) may adjust the number
of credits for courses based on actual needs.
Article 16. Physical education courses consist of four required courses, which are non-credit-
bearing. After completing the required courses, any additional courses beyond the fourth
will be counted as one credit each. However, physical education courses are not included
in the minimum graduation credit requirements for any department or degree program.
For undergraduate degree students enrolled in dual degree programs at the university, the
regulations of their respective units shall apply if they have specific guidelines for taking
physical education courses.
Military education (Nursing) courses are electives for freshmen, sophomores, and juniors,
### Page 8
and are credit-bearing, but the credits do not count toward the minimum graduation credit
requirements for any department or degree program.
Article 17. The total number of credits that students of each department (degree program, institute)
may take per semester shall comply with the University Act and the National Taipei
University Course Selection Regulations.
Chapter 4 Registration and Course Selection
Article 18. Except for Freshmen, all students must complete registration at the beginning of each
semester on the specified dates; the procedure shall be determined separately.
Article 19. Students who are unable to complete registration within the first week of classes must
apply for a registration leave in advance. Upon approval, they may extend the registration
deadline to the makeup registration date specified in the academic calendar (inclusive of
the date). Those who fail to apply for leave or do not register by the approved leave date,
unless granted an extension by the Academic Affairs Office or the Extension Education
Center shall be required to take a leave of absence and complete the necessary procedures
within the prescribed timeframe. Students who fail to apply for a leave of absence within
the allotted time or fail to register after the leave of absence period has expired shall be
dismissed from the university.
Article 20. Students are required to pay all applicable fees according to the prescribed standards
at the time of registration each semester.
Students who have completed the registration process but fail to pay the required credit
fees as stipulated, after receiving two notices from the university via written and email
communication, will not be allowed to register for the following semester. For graduating
students, the issuance of their diploma will be temporarily withheld.
For students who are unable to pay the credit fees within the designated time due to
financial difficulties or other significant reasons, they may submit a deferral application to
the academic affairs office. The application should provide an explanation of the reasons,
the payment deadline, and the method of payment, along with supporting documents. Upon
approval by the academic affairs office, the student will not be subject to the restrictions
set forth in the first part of paragraph 2.
### Page 9
Students eligible for public funding, subsidies, scholarships, tuition and fee reductions or
exemptions, or student loans must proceed in accordance with the relevant regulations.
Article 21. Students shall select courses each semester in accordance with the National Taipei
University Regulations for Student Course Selection. The regulations for course selection
shall be established separately.
Students shall not select courses with overlapping schedules; otherwise, their course
selections will be canceled.
Article 22. Students in their second year or higher of an undergraduate program or Extension
Education Bachelor program may declare a double major, minor, or various programs
within the University or at other universities. Graduate students may submit an application
for a double major program at the University or other universities, or opt for a minor at the
same level or lower level (institute, degree program) between the start of their second
semester and the end of their fourth semester. However, double majors, minors, or various
programs at other universities must not duplicate programs offered by National Taipei
University. Approval must be obtained from both the University and the external university
offering the program. Specific regulations shall be established separately. Regulations for
graduate students to take courses in various programs shall be established separately.
Article 23. The University may offer courses during summer vacation. Specific regulations shall
be established separately.
Article 24. The University may offer inter-university course selection and international course
selection based on student needs; specific regulations shall be established separately. The
regulations for international course selection shall be implemented after approval by the
Academic Affairs Conference and the University Council. The regulations for inter-
university course selection shall take effect from the date of announcement after being
approved by the Academic Affairs Conference and the University Council; the same
applies to any amendments.
Article 24.1 Outstanding student-athletes at National Taipei University who are selected for
training at the National Sports Training Center (hereinafter referred to as the “NSTC”) must
first complete their registration and course selection procedures at the University before
### Page 10
enrolling in specialized courses offered by the NSTC.
The specialized courses mentioned above shall be planned by an inter-university
curriculum committee, convened by the NSTC and representatives from the students'
original university. This committee will assist in managing administrative tasks such as
course credits, instructor arrangements, and grade submissions.
Chapter 5 Absence from Class and Meeting
Article 25. Students who are unable to attend classes due to illness or other reasons should request
leave from their course instructor. Those who do not attend required gatherings should
apply for leave through the Student Affairs Office. Regulations regarding student absences
shall be established separately.
Article 26. Students are considered to be on leave if their leave application has been approved and
they do not attend class. Any absence from class for which leave has not been applied or
approved shall be regarded as truancy.
Article 27. Students are considered to be absent if their leave application has been approved and
they do not attend the required gatherings. Any absence from gatherings for which leave
has not been applied for or approved shall be regarded as an unauthorized absence.
Article 28. The course instructor will determine the grades for the missed classes at their
discretion. Students whose total absences amount to one-third of the total class hours for
all courses taken in the current semester will be required to take a leave of absence.
Article 29. Each hour of unexcused absence in a course will be counted as three hours. Students
whose total unexcused absences across all courses in a semester reach 45 hours will be
dismissed from the University.
Article 30. For each absence from a required on-campus gathering, one point will be deducted
from the conduct grade for the semester, and three points will be deducted for each absence
from a required off-campus gathering. Any unexcused absence from on-campus or off-
campus assemblies will result in double the points being deducted.
Article 31. Absences due to official leave, bereavement leave for immediate family members or
spouse, or leaves granted to students for reasons of pregnancy, miscarriage, childbirth, or
caring for children under the age of three will not result in deductions from their grades.
### Page 11
However, if the bereavement leave exceeds one week, it shall be handled in accordance
with Article 28.
If the total hours of absence due to approved leaves for pregnancy, miscarriage, childbirth,
or caring for children under the age of three exceed one-third of the total class hours for
the semester, the instructor may provide a make-up class, supplementary exams, or other
remedial measures as needed.
Absences due to illness with a medical certificate will result in a deduction of half a point.
Chapter 6 Transfer and Change Departments (Institutes)
Article 32. When there are remaining quotas for sophomores or juniors in the Bachelor's Programs
and Extension Education Bachelor's Program, transfer students may be recruited according
to the University's transfer student admission rules. Recruitment is limited to vacancies
resulting from admissions or withdrawals and does not include those due to deferred
admission, leaves of absence, or additional quotas.
Transfer admissions are conducted in accordance with the University’s regulations.
Students who are admitted through the University’s transfer examination may transfer to
the University for their studies.
The eligibility of applicants for the transfer examination, based on whether the academic
program or credits they previously pursued align with the nature of the intended department
or degree program, shall be determined by the respective department or degree program
and confirmed by the Admissions Committee before being included in the admissions
guidelines.
Students who are dismissed due to failing conduct grades are not eligible to apply for the
University’s transfer examination.
Article 33. Undergraduate program students and Extension Education Bachelor program students
may transfer between programs upon passing the examination.
Article 34. (Deleted)
Article 35. (Deleted)
Article 36. Transfer students may be exempted from required courses and credits if they have
already passed them at their previous university before transferring. After transferring,
### Page 12
required courses and credits that have been passed at the previous university, or passed
through an examination may also be exempted. However, from the transfer year onward,
the minimum number of required credits per semester shall not be reduced. The regulations
for credit exemptions will be stipulated separately.
Article 37. Undergraduate and Extension Education Bachelor program students who have
completed first-year courses and wish to transfer to another university must apply for a
transfer with the admission notification from the receiving university. For minors, a written
statement from a parent or guardian is required. Upon approval, a transfer certificate will
be issued. However, once the transfer process is completed, students may not request to
return to the original university.
Article 38. Students from each department (degree program or institute) may apply for a transfer
to another department (degree program or institute) in accordance with the following
regulations, with a limit of one transfer. Once the transfer has been approved, students are
not allowed to request another transfer to a different department (degree program or
institute) or return to their original department (institute).
I. Undergraduate students who submit their application before the beginning of
their second academic year may apply to transfer into the second year of a
bachelor program offered by any department or degree program.
II. Extension Education Bachelor program students who submit their application
before the beginning of their second academic year may apply to transfer into the
second year of any department or degree program in the Extension Education
Bachelor program.
III. Undergraduate students who submit their application before the beginning of
their third academic year may apply to transfer into the third year of a minor,
double major, or a similar department or degree program in the undergraduate
division, or into the second year of a department or degree program of a different
nature.
IV. Extension Education Bachelor program students who submit their application
before the beginning of their third academic year may apply to transfer into the
### Page 13
third year of a minor, double major, or a similar department or degree program in
the Extension Education Bachelor program, or into the second year of a
department or degree program of a different nature.
V. Undergraduate students who submit their application for special reasons before
the beginning of their fourth academic year may apply to transfer into the third
year of a minor, double major, or a similar department or degree program in the
undergraduate division.
VI. Extension Education Bachelor program students who submit their application for
special reasons before the beginning of their fourth academic year may apply to
transfer into the third year of a minor, double major, or a similar department or
degree program within the Extension Education Bachelor program.
VII. (Deleted)
VIII. Extension Education Bachelor program students in higher grades who apply for
a transfer may request to transfer into an appropriate year of a similar department
or degree program, or minor, based on the courses and credits they have already
completed.
IX. Students who transfer within the same department shall be subject to the
provisions specified in the prior paragraph.
X. The quota for transfer students in both the Undergraduate and Extension
Education Bachelor programs shall not exceed 20% of the originally approved
and allocated admission quota for the department or degree program in each
respective year.
XI. For students who have transferred to a lower year of study as a result, the years
of repeated enrollment will not be counted toward the maximum study period in
the receiving department.
XII. The regulations for graduate students transferring to another department (institute)
shall be stipulated separately in the graduate program guidelines.
Article 39. Students applying for a transfer of department (institute) must submit an application
form, along with transcripts for all semesters and the required documents for the intended
### Page 14
department or degree program, within the deadline specified in the academic calendar for
the second semester of each academic year. The application must be approved by a parent
or guardian (applicable only to students under the age of 18) and by the current department
(degree program or institute), after which it will be submitted to the intended department
(degree program or institute) for review. Following departmental review, the result will be
forwarded to the Office of Academic Affairs or the Extension Education Center for
consolidation and submitted to the President for final approval and announcement.
Each department (degree program, institute) may conduct examinations for students
applying for transfer when deemed necessary, using the exam results as a reference for
determining whether the application is approved.
Regulations for transfer students shall be formulated separately.
Chapter 7 Leave of Absence/Reenrollment, Dismissals, and Expulsion
Article 40. If a student is unable to continue their studies due to personal reasons or illness, they
must submit documentary proof when applying for a leave of absence. Underage students
must also provide a letter of consent from their parent or guardian. If the leave of absence
is due to illness, a diagnosis certificate issued by the University's physician or regional
hospital under the National Health Insurance Administration must be submitted with the
application.
A leave of absence certificate will be issued only after the student's application for leave of
absence is approved and the student has completed the procedures for leaving the
University.
Article 41. Undergraduate and Extension Education Bachelor program students must submit their
leave of absence applications before the final exams of the current semester. Graduate
students must submit their leave of absence applications before the end of the current
semester. The leave of absence may be granted for one semester, one academic year, or two
academic years, with a cumulative maximum of two academic years as the general
principle, starting from the semester in which the leave of absence begins. If students need
to apply for further leave of absence due to severe illness, mental illness, or special
circumstances, they may be granted one additional extension, not exceeding two academic
### Page 15
years, upon approval by the head of their department (degree program, institute) and the
Dean of Academic Affairs or the Director of the Extension Education Center. However,
students who are conscripted for military service, pregnant, giving birth, or caring for
children under the age of three during the leave of absence must submit a copy of the draft
notice, a medical certificate issued by a contracted regional hospital or higher under the
National Health Insurance Administration, a household registration transcript, or other
relevant documents to apply for an extension of the leave. Upon the conclusion of the leave,
students must submit a copy of the discharge certificate or a medical certificate issued by
a contracted regional hospital or higher under the National Health Insurance Administration
to apply for reenrollment. The period of military service, pregnancy, childbirth, or childcare
for children under the age of three will not be counted towards the cumulative leave of
absence period.
Freshmen who apply for a leave of absence due to participation in the 'Youth Education
and Employment Savings Account Program' may take a leave of absence for up to three
academic years, which will be counted separately from the maximum period allowed for
regular deferrals and suspensions.
Article 42. Students who meet any of the following conditions shall take a leave of absence. The
University will issue a notice and mandate the completion of the school-leaving process
within a specified deadline.
I. Students who fail to complete the registration process after the approved
registration deferment period has expired.
II. Students who fail to select courses or fail to meet the minimum credit
requirements for the semester after the course add/drop period has expired.
III. Students whose total absences in a semester reach one-third of the total class
hours for the current semester.
IV. Students certified by a regional hospital or medical center under the National
Health Insurance Administration as having an illness that is improbable to
improve in the short term, or who are identified as posing a risk to public health,
shall be granted a leave of absence.
### Page 16
V. Students who are absent from all final examinations and fail to take the make-up
examinations on the specified date.
VI. Students who are subject to take a leave of absence in accordance with the
regulations governing student rewards and penalties.
Article 43. Students on leave of absence must apply for reenrollment before the expiration of the
leave period. Those who took a leave due to illness are required to submit a health
certificate issued by the university’s physician or a contracted regional hospital under the
National Health Insurance Administration, and may only reenroll in their studies upon
approval.
Article 44. Students reenrolling in their studies after a leave of absence shall be placed in the
academic year or semester of the original department (degree program or institute).
However, those who took a leave of absence mid-semester must return to the same
academic year or semester they were previously enrolled in.
Students should be guided to reenroll in an appropriate department (degree program or
institute) if the original department (degree program or institute) is altered or discontinued.
Article 45. If a student does not resume studies after the leave of absence period expires and does
not apply for an extension of the leave of absence period, the student shall be dismissed
from the university.
Article 46. Students who meet any of the following conditions shall be dismissed. The university
will notify them and require them to complete the school-leaving process within a specified
period.
I. (Deleted)
II. A failing grade in Conduct.
III. Students who fail to complete registration procedures after the extension of the
registration deadline, or fail to select courses after the course add/drop deadline,
and do not complete the leave of absence procedures by the specified deadline
after receiving a final written notice.
IV. Students who do not reenroll in studies after their leave of absence period expires
and do not apply for an extension.
### Page 17
V. Students found by the University to have engaged in impersonation during an
examination.
VI. Students with 45 hours of unexcused absences within a semester.
VII. Students who have received failing grades for half of the total credits in a
semester, cumulatively for two terms.
VIII. The student failed to meet the requirements specified in Article 72, Paragraph 1
of this academic regulation by the time their period of study expired.
IX. Students who are subject to dismissal in accordance with the regulations
governing student rewards and penalties.
X. Students who are absent from all final examinations and fail to take the make-up
examinations on the specified date.
XI. Students who are absent from all final examinations and fail to take the make-up
examinations on the specified date.
XII. (Deleted)
XIII. Graduate students:
(I). Master's and on-the-job graduate program students who take more than 9
credits in a semester and receive failing grades for half or more of the total
credits taken in that semester for two cumulative semesters.
(II). Students in the on-the-job graduate program who are overseas Chinese
students, Mainland Chinese students, international students, Mongolian and
Tibetan students repatriated for higher education, indigenous Taiwanese
students, students with a disability ID card, and students who are the
offspring of government personnel stationed abroad, who take more than 9
credits in a semester and receive failing grades for two-thirds or more of the
total credits in that semester for two cumulative semesters.
(III). Students who fail the doctoral qualifying examination and fail again after a
retake.
(IV). Students who fail the thesis/dissertation defense and are ineligible for a
retake; or those who meet the retake requirements but still fail after retaking
### Page 18
the examination.
(V). Students who have been conferred degrees found to involve forgery,
falsification, plagiarism, ghostwriting, or other misconduct related to their
thesis or dissertation, work(s), certificate of achievement, written report,
technical report, or professional practice report shall have their degrees
revoked, with the revocation of the diploma publicly announced. The
student will be notified to return the diploma, and the revocation and
annulment will be reported to other colleges, universities, and relevant
authorities or institutions.
XIV. Students in the Reserve Officer Training Corps program who were admitted
through additional slots and withdrew from training in their unit.
Students who believe the decision of dismissal to be unlawful or inappropriate
and in violation of their rights may present the relevant supporting documents
and file an appeal in accordance with the University's Student Appeal Regulations.
Before the result of the appeal is confirmed, students under disciplinary action
may continue their studies at NTPU. However, should the appeal be rejected,
any grades received after the appeal is filed and before the result is confirmed
shall not be recognized.
A student under disciplinary action who does not obtain relief through an internal
appeal may pursue a legal appeal or administrative lawsuit in accordance with
the law. If the original decision is deemed unlawful or inappropriate by a decision
of the competent authority or a ruling of the administrative court, the University
shall issue a new decision accordingly.
Students who are permitted to resume studies in accordance with the preceding
provision but are unable to do so promptly due to special circumstances may
retroactively apply for a leave of absence, which will not be counted toward the
maximum duration of leave allowed.
Article 47. Students who have withdrawn from the University and have completed one or more
semesters of study with grades shall be awarded a Certificate of Study.
### Page 19
Article 48. Students shall be expelled from NTPU and will not receive any academic certification
if they meet any of the following conditions. The University will notify them to complete
the school-leaving process within a specified period.
I. Students who submit documentation that is found to be the product of forgery,
falsification, impersonation, or substitution shall be expelled.
II. Students who violate University regulations in a serious manner or engage in
misconduct that contravenes the law shall be expelled.
III. Students who are subject to be expelled in accordance with the regulations
governing student rewards and penalties.
Chapter 8 Examinations and Grades
Article 49. The following examinations are conducted each semester at the University:
I. Quizzes: Conducted by course instructors at any time.
II. Midterm examination: Conducted each semester according to the calendar.
III. Final examination: Conducted each semester according to the calendar.
Article 50. Students who are unable to attend midterm or final examinations due to serious illness
or significant accidents must submit a leave request. Once the leave is granted, they will
not be allowed to take any exams scheduled for that day and must wait for a make-up exam.
Students who have been granted approved leave but still attend examinations on the
designated leave date shall have their exam scores voided and will still be required to take
the make-up exams.
Article 51. Students who fail to attend scheduled examinations without an approved leave or
whose leave requests are not granted shall receive a score of zero for the missed subjects.
Article 52. Students who violate examination regulations shall be dealt with in accordance with
the University's examination regulations, which shall be established separately.
Article 53. The regulations governing graduate students' studies and degree examinations shall be
established separately and submitted to the Ministry of Education for reference.
Article 54. Students’ grades consist of academic performance and conduct.
Article 55. The academic grades for undergraduate and Extension Education Bachelor program
students are categorized into semester grades and final grade averages, with a maximum
### Page 20
score of 100 and a passing score of 60. For graduate students , the passing score is 70.
Semester grades for courses taken by students require approval from the department (or
institute) and may be assessed on a 'Pass' or 'Fail' basis. For graduate students enrolled in
undergraduate-level courses, the passing score is 60.
Article 56. The quizzes grade for each course, including quizzes, notes, book reports, essays, and
practice scores, shall be combined with the midterm exam score to calculate the overall
class performance grade. This grade shall be determined by the course instructor based on
the weighted proportions of each score assigned throughout the course.
The semester grade for each course shall be calculated by combining the daily performance
grade and the final exam grade. For courses with laboratory or internship components, the
semester grade shall be determined by averaging the daily performance grade, final exam
grade, and the laboratory or internship grade.
The semester grade for each course shall be rounded to the nearest whole number. However,
if a student fails the laboratory or internship component, the entire course must be retaken.
Article 57. The semester grade for each course shall be calculated by the course instructor in
accordance with Article 56 of these regulations, recorded in the grade register, and
submitted to the Office of Academic Affairs or the Extension Education Center for
registration and preservation.
For courses co-taught by two or more instructors, the semester grade shall be determined
collectively by all instructors. This grade shall be recorded in the grade register and
submitted to the Office of Academic Affairs or the Extension Education Center for
registration and preservation.
The regulations for submitting grades by course instructors shall be established separately.
Article 58. Once instructors have submitted students' grades to the Office of Academic Affairs or
the Extension Education Center, grades may only be changed for reasons not attributable
to the students. The regulations governing grade corrections by instructors shall be
established separately.
Undergraduate and Extension Education Bachelor program students may review their
semester grades through the responsible academic affairs unit or the University’s online
### Page 21
grade inquiry system after the conclusion of end-of-semester examinations. The review
period begins on the day following the grade submission deadline for instructors and ends
on the day before the “Start of Classes” date for the subsequent semester, as specified in
the university calendar.
If a student has questions about a grade, they may submit an inquiry to the responsible
academic affairs unit. If it is confirmed that the recorded grade matches the grade submitted
by the course instructor, and the student still has concerns, they shall contact the course
instructor directly for further clarification.
Article 59. The semester grade point average (GPA) for students is calculated by dividing the total
grade points earned during the semester by the total number of credits completed. The
result shall be rounded to the nearest first decimal place.
Article 60. The calculation of the semester grade average shall include courses with a failing grade.
Course graded with a pass/fail grading system shall not include in the calculation of the
academic grade point average.
Students shall not earn credits for courses in which they receive a non-passing or failing
grade.
Article 61. The semester grade is calculated by combining students’ regular performance grades
with their final examination grades. The semester academic grade point average is
determined by dividing the total grade points earned by the total number of credits taken
during the semester. The cumulative academic GPA is calculated by dividing the total grade
points earned across all semesters by the total number of credits taken.
The academic grade point average (GPA) of bachelor's degree graduates from each
department and degree program shall constitute their final grade averages.
The final grade averages for graduate students shall be determined by calculating the sum
of the grade received for the thesis/dissertation defense and the overall grade average.
Article 62. For students enrolled in (or before) Academic year 2021-2022 and transfer students in
the academic year 2022-2023, the semester grading scale regulation is specified as follows:
I. Grade A: A score of 80 or above is equivalent to a GPA of 4.0.
II. Grade B: A score above 70 and below 80 is equivalent to a GPA of 3.0.
### Page 22
III. Grade C: A score above 60 and below 70 is equivalent to a GPA of 2.0.
IV. Grade D: A score above 50 and below 60 is equivalent to a GPA of 1.0.
V. Grade E: A score of 49 or below is equivalent to a GPA of 0.
Starting from the academic year 2021-2022, the conversion of new students' academic
performance into grade points average shall be conducted in accordance with the
university's “Guidelines for the Conversion of Student Academic Performance into
Grade Points,” which will be established separately.
Article 63. Students will not receive credit for courses in which they fail during the semester. If
the course is a required course, they must retake it.
Article 64. Students who receive failing grades for one-half of the total credits in a semester and
accumulate this result twice shall be dismissed from the university.
Article 65. For overseas Chinese students, Mainland Chinese students, international students,
Mongolian and Tibetan students repatriated for higher education, Indigenous Taiwanese
students, students who are the offspring of government personnel stationed abroad, and
distinguished athletes meeting Ministry of Education criteria, failure in courses totaling
two-thirds of their credits for two cumulative semesters shall be dismissed from the
University.
Article 65.1 The preceding two articles are not applicable to bachelor program students who have
a disability certificate (manual), students who have been identified by the Ministry of
Education, municipal or county (city) governments as special education students and hold
a special education student certificate issued by the University's counseling committee, and
students taking the Post-baccalaureate Expertise Development Program.
Article 66. Excluding physical education and military education, the limitations outline in Article
64 and Article 65 of these Regulations shall not apply to students who have taken fewer
than 9 credits in the semester.
Article 67. (Deleted)
Article 68. Make-up examinations for midterm and final examinations shall be arranged by the
course instructor. However, the timelines for submitting and amending grades must comply
with the “National Taipei University Regulations on Submission and Amendment by
### Page 23
Course Instructors.”
Article 69. Students who are granted make-up examinations due to official leave, funeral leave
for immediate family members or a spouse, pregnancy, miscarriage, childbirth, raising
children under the age of three, or hospitalization due to severe illness shall have their
grades determined based on their actual performance. For students granted make-up
examinations for other approved reasons, any portion of their scores exceeding 60 points
will be calculated at 80% of the original score.
Article 70. Students who are required to take make-up examinations but fail to attend on the
designated date shall receive a grade of zero.
Article 71. If students granted examination leave are unable to attend the final examinations and
fail to take the make-up examinations on the designated date in the following semester,
their leave of absence shall be retroactively applied from the previous semester if the
absence covers all subjects.
Chapter 9 Graduation and Degrees
Article 72. Students who have completed the required program duration and meet the following
criteria shall be granted graduation.
I. The sentence is grammatically correct, and the verb tenses are consistent. It
effectively uses the present tense (“fulfill,” “complete,” “comply”) for conditions
or requirements that apply generally.
II. Students who meet the standards set forth in the Regulations for the Foreign
Language Proficiency Examination (applicable to all undergraduate students
starting from the 2020-2021 academic year). However, those who passed the
English Proficiency Graduation Qualification Examination Guidelines prior to
the 2019-2020 academic year (inclusive) are exempt from this requirement.
III. Students who meet the required passing grade in conduct.
IV. Students who meet other relevant graduation requirements set by their
department or institute.
V. Starting from the 2021-2022 academic year, undergraduate students are required
to complete and obtain qualifications in at least one of the following: a micro-
### Page 24
program, a credit program, a minor, or a double major offered by the university
or within the University System of Taipei, or complete the pre-service teacher
education program. Students who apply and earn credits by completing a micro-
program or credit program in accordance with the university's Regulations for
Students Taking Academic Programs and the University System of Taipei Inter-
Institutional Credit Programs and Micro-program Regulations may have these
credits counted toward their graduation requirements.
Regulations and standards specified in Subparagraph 2 of the preceding
paragraph shall be established by the Office of Academic Affairs and
implemented upon approval by the Academic Affairs Meeting and the University
Council. These provisions apply to the university's undergraduate students who
are not enrolled in the Department of Foreign Languages and Applied Linguistics;
however, they do not apply to students with disabilities or international students.
The regulations related to Subparagraph 4 of the preceding paragraph for each
department (degree program, or institute) shall be approved by the Department
Affairs Meeting and implemented upon the President's approval.
Regulations for the review of students' graduation qualifications shall be
established separately.
Article 73. Students in each department and degree program who are required to complete
internships during vacation periods, in accordance with the regulations, must do so at
suitable locations, either on or off campus during the winter or summer vacations of their
second or third academic year. Students who fail to submit an internship report and an
internship completion certificate shall not be eligible for graduation. The regulations
governing student internships during vacation periods shall be established separately.
Article 74. Undergraduate and Extension Education Bachelor program graduates shall be
conferred a bachelor's degree by the University in accordance with the Degree Conferral
Act. For students completing the Post-Baccalaureate Expertise Development Program, the
designation “Post-Baccalaureate Expertise” shall be noted on their degree.
Students in the master's and doctoral programs of each department (or institute) who meet
### Page 25
the requirements of the Degree Conferral Act and Article 72 of the Academic Regulations
and pass the degree examination shall be conferred a master's or doctoral degree by the
University in accordance with the Degree Conferral Act.
Article 74.1 The University may collaborate with overseas universities to offer transnational dual
degree programs. The relevant regulations shall be approved by the Academic Affairs
Meeting and shall take effect on the date of promulgation; the same applies to amendments.
Article 74.2 The master’s thesis or doctoral dissertation for students enrolled in master’s and
doctoral programs related to the arts, applied technology, or athletics may be substituted
with alternative works, proof of achievements accompanied by a written report, or a
technical report. The standards for determination and the required documents for each
category shall be established by the respective department (degree program, institute) and
college. These standards must be equivalent to those of a thesis or dissertation and must
receive approval from both the department (degree program, institute) affairs meeting and
the college affairs meeting. They shall be submitted to the Academic Affairs Meeting for
final approval before taking effect and must be published in the information disclosure
section of the University’s website.
For master's programs focused on professional practice, students may substitute their
master's thesis with a professional practice report. The standards for recognizing
professional practice shall be handled in accordance with the previously established
provisions.
The scope of recognition, data format, content requirements, and other related guidelines
for substituting a master’s thesis or doctoral dissertation with works, proof of achievements,
written reports, or technical reports for the categories specified in the preceding two
paragraphs, or substituting a master’s thesis with a professional practice report in master’s
programs, shall be governed by the Ministry of Education’s “Regulations Governing the
Determination of Procedures for Naming Degrees, Degree Conferral Criteria, and
Alternatives to Submission of a Master’s Thesis or Doctoral Dissertation.”
Article 74.3 The Chinese and English names of degrees, degree conferral requirements, and other
related regulations for each department (degree program, institute) and college shall be
### Page 26
proposed by the respective department (degree program, institute) and college. The
regulations shall be approved by the department (degree program, institute) affairs council
meeting and the college affairs council meeting, and submitted to the Academic Affairs
Conference for review and approval before being officially announced and implemented.
Article 74.4 The Chinese and English names of degrees for each department (degree program,
institute) and college, along with the implementation year, conferral requirements, and
other related regulations shall be published on the university website in the section
designated for public access to university information.
The degree conferral requirements outlined in the previous paragraph specify whether
works, proof of achievements, and the written report, technical report, or professional
practice report may be submitted as substitutes for the master's thesis or doctoral
dissertation.
Article 74.5 The degree certificate shall contain the student’s name, date of birth, college, institute,
department, degree program, division, graduation date, degree title, and certificate number.
Additionally, for students who have completed a minor or double major at the University
or another institution, the names of the institution and department must also be noted. For
reissued certificates, the date of reissuance must be specified as well.
Chapter 10 Supplementary Provisions
Article 75. Students who are enrolled or on a leave of absence and violate university regulations
or engage in other misconduct shall be subject to disciplinary actions commensurate with
the severity of the offense. These actions may include warnings, reprimands, demerits,
enforced suspension, dismissal, or expulsion. The university’s regulations governing
student rewards and penalties shall be stipulated separately and submitted to the Ministry
of Education for record.
Article 76. The regulations shall be approved by the University Council Meeting, submitted to
the Ministry of Education for record, and shall take effect on the date of promulgation; the
same applies to amendments.

Note:
I. The “Dual Instructor System” instructional courses are primarily taught by full-time faculty members,
supplemented by collaborative teaching with experts from industry, government, and academia. Full-time
faculty members are required to be present for the entire semester to lead the course instruction, and their
hourly rate is determined in accordance with the “National Taipei University Regulations for the
Calculation of Faculty Members' Teaching Hours and Overtime Payment.”
II. Each course may invite experts from industry, government, or academia to deliver lectures or speeches
during class sessions. The total number of these sessions shall not exceed nine times within the semester,
with each session lasting at least 50 minutes. However, if the course has specific requirements, the
approval from the department's curriculum committee exempts the course from the aforementioned
limitation on the number of sessions.
III. Experts from industry, government, or academia who are from a distance of 30 kilometers (inclusive) or
more may be reimbursed for necessary transportation expenses based on actual circumstances, in
accordance with the guidelines outlined in the “Domestic Business Travel Expense Reimbursement.”
IV. If there are any other special circumstances, please submit for the principal's approval and proceed
accordingly.

國立臺北大學數位自學課程作業要點
111年3月23日第57次教務會議通過
113年10月9日第62次教務會議通過修正第二、三條條文
一、本校為推動自主學習，提供多元學習管道，鼓勵學生修習數位自學課程，特訂定「國立
臺北大學數位自學課程作業要點」（以下簡稱本要點）。
二、本要點所稱數位自學課程，係指由國內外知名大專校院及企業機構於國際線上課程平臺
（如：Coursera、edX、FutureLearn、AWS Educate）所開設之數位課程，如為前述以外
之數位自學課程適合學生修讀者，由教學單位事先專簽提出申請。
三、相關業務單位權責：
(一) 教學單位：
1.新增課程：各系（中心）、院得視課程規劃需求選定數位自學課程，設定修課對
象、訂定學分數及審查方式，並檢附「專業科目規劃表」及「數位自學課程資料
表」，提請課程委員會經三級三審通過後，列為系（中心）、院之必修或專業選
修，並辦理學生學分認列申請及審查事宜。
2.異動或刪除課程：各系（中心）、院若異動或刪除數位自學課程，需檢附「專業
科目異動表」，提請課程委員會經三級三審通過。數位自學課程終止認列前，應
規劃已申請數位自學課程修課學生學分認列的配套措施，應持續保障已申請修課
學生且符合認列條件者於畢業前皆可認列學分，或規劃學分抵認替代方案。
(二) 教務處：制定數位自學法規、訂定補助獎勵機制、公告數位自學課程及施行辦法、
辦理學分認列事宜等。
四、課程學分認列方式：
(一) 數位自學課程畢業學分之認列，依各系（中心）、院規定辦理，以認列畢業學分 6
學分為上限。各系（中心）、院另有更嚴格規定者，從其規範。
(二) 經同意認列之數位自學課程及學分數，將於成績單註記「通過」，不計算學期
GPA，亦不計入畢業 GPA。
(三) 同一數位自學課程以認列一次為限，不得重複認列學分。
(四) 學生入學後在本校已修習之課程，不得以相同名稱之線上課程辦理學分認列。
(五) 學生取得課程修課證明日期若為入學前，得依本校學生抵免學分辦法申請抵免；
日期若為退學後或畢業後，不得辦理該科目之認列。
(六) 學生欲修習數位自學課程者，應事先提出修課申請，於每學期加退選結束前填具
「數位自學課程修課申請表」，經開課單位及所屬系所審核用印後，繳交申請表
正本至課務組備查，否則不予認列學分。
(七) 學生修課完成後，應檢具完課證明或修課證書(Certificate)，於規定期限內向所屬
系（中心）、院申請學分認列審查，再由各系（中心）、院將審查結果統一於每
學期期末考週送交註冊組辦理學分認列。
五、經費補助：
(一) 當年度通過數位自學課程審查之系（中心）、院，可申請一次業務費補助，最多
可申請 3次，相同課程續開不可重複申請補助。
### Page 3
(二) 若系所決議課程學分審定採教師評核（筆試或實體作業）形式，另就每一申請學分
採計之學生，補助負責教師講座鐘點費新臺幣 1,000 元。每位教師每學期就相同課
程，至多申請補助新臺幣5,000元。
(三) 補助經費由教育部高教深耕計畫經費或相關計畫經費支應，實際補助額度將視該
年度經費多寡而定。
六、本要點現行適用對象為學士班學生，將視辦理成效，再決定擴大適用對象。
七、本要點如有未盡事宜，悉依教育部與本校相關規定辦理。
八、本要點經教務會議通過，陳請校長核定後公布實施，修正時亦同。

國立臺北大學遠距教學實施辦法
本校90年5月30日第3次教務會議通過
本校95年3月14日第14次教務會議將「國立臺北大學遠距教學試行辦法」修正為「國立臺北大學遠距教學實施辦法」，
並修正通過相關條文
本校95年10月18日第15次教務會議修正第1、3條條文
本校98年3月19日第24次教務會議修正第9條條文
本校105年10月12日第46次教務會議修正第1、2、3、7、9條，並新增第9條之1
本校108年11月20日第52次教務會議延續會修正通過第4條
本校111年10月12日第58次教務會議修正通過第2、3、12、13條，並新增第8、9、10、11條
本校112年3月22日第59次教務會議修正通過第2、3、13條條文
本校113年3月20日第61次教務會議修正通過第3條並新增第7條之1條文，自113學年度起實施
本校113年10月9日第62次教務會議修正通過第4條
第一條 依據教育部「專科以上學校遠距教學實施辦法」之規定，特訂定「國立臺北大學遠
距教學實施辦法」(以下簡稱本辦法)。
第二條 本辦法所稱遠距教學，指師生透過通訊網路、電腦網路、視訊頻道等傳輸媒體進行
教學，並產生互動教學行為。
遠距教學方式包括同步（即時視訊）遠距教學、非同步（網路）遠距教學及同步與
非同步混合式遠距教學，其實施遠距教學時，應於具備教學實施、記錄學生學習情
形及其他支援學習功能之學習管理系統為之，並應符合本校教學標準。
本辦法所稱遠距教學課程，指單一科目授課時數二分之一以上以遠距教學方式進行
之課程。
本辦法所稱同步與非同步混合式遠距教學，係指遠距教學課程中同時提供同步及非
同步遠距教學，且同步（即時視訊）或實體授課時數至少達二分之一以上者。
第三條 首次採遠距教學方式授課之課程，或曾採遠距學方式授課但擬修訂教學計畫內容之
課程，各學系（學位學程、所、室、中心）應於開課前一學期，檢具「教學計畫
書」及「遠距教學課程品質檢核表」提出申請，經三級（系、院、校）課程委員會
審議通過後，始得開授；另為控管遠距課程開課教學品質，續開時應於開課前一學
期依課程所屬學制提供「遠距教學課程續開表」，經各學系(學位學程、所、室、中
心)課程委員會審議通過後，送校課程委員會作工作報告備查。
前項教學計畫書應包含教學目標、適合修讀對象、課程大綱、上課方式、師生互動
討論、作業繳交、成績評量方式及上課注意事項。
第四條 本校畢業學生選修遠距教學之課程以不超過畢業總學分數二分之一為限。另每學期
除延修生外，以不超過修習總學分數二分之一為限。
本校學生採計為畢業總學分數之遠距教學課程學分數，已超過畢業總學分數之三分
之一而未超過二分之一者，學校應將遠距教學課程開設及品質確保之相關規定報教
育部審查核准後，始得開設。
學生以遠距教學方式取得之推廣教育學分申請學分抵免，其課程學分數已超過畢業
總學分數之三分之一者，學校應造冊報教育部備查。
學生修習教育部「臺灣大專院校人工智慧學程聯盟(TAICA)」課程之相關選課及學
分採認規範，另依「國立臺北大學 AI學程聯盟選課須知」辦理。
### Page 3
第五條 本校學生欲選修主播學校為本校或他校所開設之遠距教學課程者，須於本校選課期
間按照本校選課方式辦理選課，上課時間以主播學校為主。
第六條 (刪除)
第七條 (刪除)
第七條之一 授課教師應於課程結束一個月內完成「遠距課程自我評鑑報告」，由開課單位
於次一學期送所屬課程委員會議審評後，送教務處備查。
遠距教學課程，比照本校一般教學課程實施教學評鑑；其期末教學滿意度分數
未達 3.5分者，授課教師另應提出「遠距課程教學改善計畫」，再依第三條第一
項規定之續開程序審議通過後，該課程始得續開。
前開二項自我評鑑報告及課程教學改善計畫應作為課程評鑑及續開課程之參
據，且至少保存五年，供日後接受教育部評鑑訪視時之參考。
第八條 教師實施遠距教學課程，授課時數應符合大學法施行細則之學分計算規定。全學期
教學內容大綱、教材、師生互動記錄、評量記錄、學生全程上課記錄及作業報告
等，於課程結束後，應置於本校數位學習平台，至少保存五年，供日後成績查詢、
教學評鑑或接受訪視之參考。
第九條 教師開授遠距教學課程時，教師應將教材置於本校數位學習平台或符合教育部課程
認證功能平台，平台應具備紀錄課程內容、教學進度、學習評量、師生互動交流、
作業繳交、系統使用說明等情形；且應同時開闢網路討論區，或提供電子郵件帳號
及其他聯絡管道，供修課學生進行教學上之雙向溝通。
第十條 同步視訊教學上課期間遇通信系統中斷或其他因素無法繼續上課時，教師應提供數
位教學檔案予收播端或另行安排補課。
第十一條 遠距教學課程授課教師得依課程需要，於教室實地舉行期中及期末考試，或以繳
交報告、作業方式，評量學生成績，並得不定期舉行平常考試。考試教室之借用
由教師逕至場地預約系統登記使用。
第十二條 教師及學生進行遠距教學課程時，除作為教學及互動外，不得作為其他用途或違
法之情事，並應遵守智慧財產權相關規定。如有涉及犯罪或侵權行為將依相關法
律規定辦理。
第十三條 採非同步（網路）遠距教學課程，如已申請本校磨課師課程者，其教師授課鐘點
時數規定適用本校磨課師課程作業要點規範；惟未申請磨課師課程之非同步遠距
教學，其教師授課時數以六週計算；上開授課時數仍受本校「授課時數及超支鐘
點費核計辦法」規範。
另每位教師每學期僅得申請開授一門(同步或同步與非同步混合式)遠距教學課程；
非同步遠距教學課程每學期得申請課程數比照本校磨課師作業要點規範辦理。
第十四條 遠距教學課程授課時數，包括課程講授、師生互動討論、測驗及其他學習活動之
時數。
第十五條 其他未盡事宜除依本校學則辦理外，並依相關法令規章處理之。
第十六條 本辦法應經教務會議通過，陳請校長核定後實施；修正時亦同。
